# THE COLLECTION
## Every Soul Has a Score

**A Ledger-Series Book · PLT Press**

*Written for the screen: 40 conversations, each scored, each an episode.*

---

### The Premise

*The Ledger* proved every conversation has a score. *The Hustle* proved every deal is a conversation. *The Collection* proves every soul is a ledger — and some ledgers get stuck.

She works for the Soul Collection Agency. Betty Plush. 222. PLT. She carries an ornate scythe, a pink collection book, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, and the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back — and she makes her rounds: the stoop, the corner store, the boardroom, the bank, the hospital, the lot, the church office, the group chat, the family table, and home again to the Collection Room. Everywhere she goes, the look changes — polka dots on the block, a tailored pink suit in the boardroom, a long teal coat at the bank — but the book never changes, and the number is never wrong.

She collects souls that are DUE. Not the dead — the stuck. People whose ledgers stopped moving years ago: a Tax never named and quietly compounding, Love withdrawn in one bad season and never redeposited, Profit taken without value and carried like it's free. A collection is not a taking. It is a conversation, scored like every conversation in this house: she opens the pink book, she reads you your own numbers — THE READING — and by the last beat the debt gets named out loud, and named debts can be paid. That payment is THE SETTLEMENT, and it is where the score jumps. She leaves every soul lighter than she found it, with a small Betty Plush soul in their hands: the receipt for a new start.

Nobody's soul is taken by force in this book. The score does the collecting.

### How the Score Works

- **P — Profit.** What a person is gaining: money, position, product, ground.
- **L — Love.** Trust, loyalty, reputation — the infrastructure every future deal stands on. It compounds over years and burns in minutes.
- **T — Tax.** What the life is costing: time, stress, risk, obligation, grief, integrity. The unnamed Tax is the expensive one.
- **PLT** moves as the three move: PLT = 14 + 0.4 × (P + L − T). It rises when Profit and Love rise and Tax falls; it falls the other way. The number is the conversation, told honestly.
- **◆ ratings** run one to five, by final PLT: ◆ under 35 · ◆◆ 35–54.9 · ◆◆◆ 55–69.9 · ◆◆◆◆ 70–84.9 · ◆◆◆◆◆ 85 and above.
- **The Reading** opens every collection: the Collector reads the soul's opening holding aloud from the pink book. **The Settlement** closes it: the debt is named, collected, and receipted with a soul.

### The Move — Every Collection Turns on One

Every soul she collects is running a play. That is the shape of this book: sketch energy on the surface — a cold open, a character who walks in larger than the room, a line you can quote back — and underneath it, one named power move per collection, priced in the open. The target tries the move: rename the cost, hoard the seat, split the price, perform the apology. The Collector answers it — not with a bigger play, but with the book. PLT is the judge: every power move gets read in Profit, in Love, and in Tax, and the score says what the move actually cost. Each conversation names its move, states its maxim in plain original words, and shows the answer. Power is just a Tax you haven't named yet.

And she has her lines. Collectors always do. Listen for them, the way the block learned to listen: *"Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."* *"The book don't blink."* And when the page turns and the debt comes off the books, one word, quiet as a stamp: *"Settled."*

### The Pink Book — Locked In

In every episode, the pink collection book is in frame — the Betty Plush book, same design, set as the series prop, the way the pink book is locked in across *The Ledger*. It does not drift, it is never renamed, and it opens in every room this book enters: on the stoop, behind the counter, across the boardroom table, at the vault, by the hospital bed, under the lot lights, on the offering table, inside the live, and at the family table. Where the debt is, the pink book is already there. Drippy rides in her arm and Spike rides on her back in every scene, and the souls she hands out as receipts are the real Betty Plush souls — named, tagged, exact: Drippy, Mendie, Denim Patch, Ember Stripe, Pixel Heart, Bell Paws, Nightwing Dawn, Listen. Never a generic plushie. Ever.

### A Note on the Scripts

Each conversation is followed by its episode script: who's in the room, the beats, the on-screen words, the score after, and the takeaway — the book's closing line, verbatim. Production follows the house rules: the dialogue is performed, the scores live on screen, the narration tells the story, and nothing publishes without Ms GCP's approval of the exact post. The **Explainer Line** in each script is a DRAFT sitting in Pope's slot — a candidate written to be replaced by Pope's own word, never presented as it.

All characters are fictional. No real brands, no real people, no real institutions named. The Collector herself is the one true person in the book, and she is exactly who the pictures say she is.

*And the rounds continue. The book you have just read is one season of rounds — forty souls, ten territories, one pink book. Tomorrow the book opens to a fresh page, because somewhere a Tax is going unnamed, somewhere Love is sitting withdrawn in an old account, somewhere a soul is due. When the score stops moving, she is already on her way. The Collection never closes.*

## CHAPTER ONE — The Hood

The Collector starts where she's known. No knocking like a stranger, no creeping like a thief — she walks the block in daylight, scythe over her shoulder like an umbrella she never needs, and every porch she passes already knows: somebody's book is about to get read. Nobody's soul is taken by force in this chapter. The score does the collecting.

### TC1.1 — The Stoop Account

*Morning on Elias's stoop, the same cracked steps he's run for forty years: the Collector arrives in her green-and-pink polka-dot fit, gold hoops catching the light, pink-and-blue hair stacked high, ornate scythe resting against the rail, Drippy the pink drip bunny plush tucked in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book in her hand — and behind her, fresh paint on the corner store shutter reads BETTY PLUSH · 222 · PLT · SOUL COLLECTION AGENCY.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · OLD MAN ELIAS (ran the stoop for 40 years, hasn't spoken to the block in three summers)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
OLD MAN ELIAS — P 45 · L 32 · T 58 │ PLT 21.6

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
OLD MAN ELIAS: "Forty years running this stoop, and now the Agency sends a girl with a garden tool."
THE COLLECTOR: "It's an ornate scythe, Elias. Garden tools don't come with their own weather."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "State your business and state it quick."
THE COLLECTOR: "Your book says P forty-five, L thirty-two, T fifty-eight. PLT twenty-one point six."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "That supposed to mean something?"
THE COLLECTOR: "It means Profit held, Love low, and a Tax so big it's got its own chair on this stoop. And baby, the chair is yours."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
OLD MAN ELIAS: P 45 (—) L 32 (—) T 55 (-3) │ PLT 22.8 ▲
*Analyst: The Reading is never an insult — it's a mirror with numbers on it. Just hearing his Tax spoken aloud shaved three points off it.*

**Beat 2 — LOVE WITHDRAWN**
OLD MAN ELIAS: "I didn't leave the block. The block got loud, got greedy, got young."
THE COLLECTOR: "Mm-hm. And three summers ago you stopped speaking. Not moved. Not died. Just — withdrew."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "A man retires his mouth when nobody's listening."
THE COLLECTOR: "A man retires his mouth, his Love goes with it, and the withdrawal never got redeposited. You know what an unredeposited withdrawal is, Elias? A debt with good posture."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "I waved at the Carter girl last week."
THE COLLECTOR: "A wave is a penny in a bank you owe a house to."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 69.2 ▲
OLD MAN ELIAS: P 45 (—) L 30 (-2) T 55 (—) │ PLT 22.0 ▼
*Analyst: She names the exact shape of it — Love wasn't lost, it was withdrawn on purpose, and withdrawals you never redeposit keep charging the account.*

**Beat 3 — THE SUMMER HE WENT QUIET**
THE COLLECTOR: "Tell me about the summer. The real line item, not the weather report."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "Boy I raised half-grown — Marcus — took my tools, pawned 'em, and the block laughed about it at the cookout. On my stoop."
THE COLLECTOR: "So the grudge has a name, and it isn't 'the block.' It's Marcus, and it's one afternoon."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "A man don't forget being laughed at in his own house."
THE COLLECTOR: "No. But a man can bill the right person. You billed everybody for one boy's tab for three years. That's not pride, Elias. That's bad bookkeeping with a nice hat."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "...He did come back that fall. Left the tools on the steps. I never said a word about that part."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book noticed. Books always notice the part we skip."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 90 (+1) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
OLD MAN ELIAS: P 47 (+2) L 30 (—) T 50 (-5) │ PLT 24.8 ▲
*Analyst: The moment the grudge gets its true name — one man, one afternoon — it stops being a tax on the whole block and shrinks to a payable size.*

**Beat 4 — THE STOOP REMEMBERS**
OLD MAN ELIAS: "And if I speak again, they just gonna act like I never left?"
THE COLLECTOR: "The stoop was never empty, Elias. Miss Odessa saves your step. The kids do your voice when you're inside. Dee brings your plate to the door and knocks twice so you can pretend you cooked."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "She knocks twice 'cause—"
THE COLLECTOR: "—'cause she knows you'll open it if it sounds like business. Love kept arriving, old man. You're the only account that stopped posting deposits."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "Forty years I kept this block's peace. Didn't think I had to apply to get back in."
THE COLLECTOR: "You don't apply. You settle, and you sit back down. There's a difference, and it's about twenty points of Tax."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 90 (—) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
OLD MAN ELIAS: P 47 (—) L 38 (+8) T 50 (—) │ PLT 28.0 ▲
*Analyst: Proof of deposits unlocks the depositor — hearing that the block never stopped loving him, his own Love line moves before he pays a cent.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Then we settle it plain. The debt is the grudge you never named — against Marcus, for one afternoon, carried three summers against everybody. Name it, and the Agency collects it. Not your soul, Elias. The grudge."
THE COLLECTOR: "Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "Marcus. I been mad at Marcus, and too proud to say a boy hurt me."
THE COLLECTOR: "There it is. The grudge is collected, signed out of your book as of this morning. Account's lighter — feel it?"
OLD MAN ELIAS: "...Steps feel lower than they did at breakfast. Like something got up out of my chair."
THE COLLECTOR: "Something did. And this is your receipt — Mendie, tagged and true, a real Betty Plush soul. Souls that mend carry the stitching on the outside. So do you, now."
OLD MAN ELIAS: "Tell the block — no. I'll tell 'em myself. At supper. Bring your book if you want witnesses."
THE COLLECTOR: "Oh, I'll be on the stoop. I hear the man who runs it is legendary."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+1) L 92 (+2) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.0 ▲
OLD MAN ELIAS: P 48 (+1) L 48 (+10) T 28 (-22) │ PLT 41.2 ▲
*Analyst: The biggest move of the day is the Tax collapse — naming the grudge and handing it over drops his Tax by twenty-two, and the Love he redeposits arrives in the same beat.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.0 · P 73 · L 92 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "THE BOOK DOES NOT BLINK"
OLD MAN ELIAS — PLT 41.2 · P 48 · L 48 · T 28 — ◆◆ "BACK ON HIS OWN STOOP"

**THE MOVE — "THE SILENT GAVEL"**
*Elias's play: holding a silent grudge as power over the whole stoop, so everybody tiptoed and his silence ran the block. The maxim: "Keep your mouth closed long enough and the whole block starts whispering around your verdict." The Collector's answer: NAME THE GRUDGE — the Reading gives the grudge its true name, Marcus, and the Settlement collects the grudge itself instead of letting it tax everybody.*

**Takeaway:** *"A grudge you won't name charges the whole block rent."*

#### Episode Script — TC1.1
**Who's in the Room:** THE COLLECTOR, soul collector for the Soul Collection Agency, scythe and pink book in hand · OLD MAN ELIAS, forty years on the stoop, three summers silent
**Beats:** 1. THE READING — the Collector opens the pink book and reads Elias's stuck holding aloud 2. LOVE WITHDRAWN — she names his three-summer silence as a withdrawal never redeposited 3. THE SUMMER HE WENT QUIET — the grudge gets its true name: Marcus, one afternoon 4. THE STOOP REMEMBERS — she proves the block never stopped making deposits 5. THE SETTLEMENT — the grudge is named and collected; Mendie is handed over as his receipt
**On-Screen Words:**
YOUR BOOK SAYS P FORTY-FIVE
I DIDN'T LEAVE THE BLOCK
THE GRUDGE HAS A NAME
THE STOOP WAS NEVER EMPTY
THE GRUDGE IS COLLECTED
**Score After:** THE COLLECTOR PLT 72.0 ◆◆◆◆ "THE BOOK DOES NOT BLINK" · OLD MAN ELIAS PLT 41.2 ◆◆ "BACK ON HIS OWN STOOP"
**The Move:** "THE SILENT GAVEL" — *"Keep your mouth closed long enough and the whole block starts whispering around your verdict."* The Collector answers with NAME THE GRUDGE.
**Takeaway:** *"A grudge you won't name charges the whole block rent."*
**Explainer Line (DRAFT — Pope's slot):** DRAFT: Elias wasn't bitter — he was billing the whole block for one boy's debt, and the Collector just corrected the invoice.

### TC1.2 — The Corner's Ledger

*Late afternoon on the corner Tre works, heat still on the pavement: the Collector steps out of the Betty Plush storefront glow in her green-and-pink polka-dot fit, gold hoops swinging, pink-and-blue hair high, ornate scythe planted like a signpost, Drippy the pink drip bunny plush in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book already open — the shop window behind her stacked with tagged souls under a BETTY PLUSH 222 sign and a PLT — PROFIT · LOVE · TAX banner.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · TRE (young runner, takes profit off the corner, puts nothing back)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
TRE — P 68 · L 28 · T 61 │ PLT 28.0

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
TRE: "Yo, if this is about a permit, I got three of 'em and none of 'em are real."
THE COLLECTOR: "Relax, baby. Permits are the city's book. I brought yours."
TRE: "I don't keep books. Books keep evidence."
THE COLLECTOR: "Your book says P sixty-eight, L twenty-eight, T sixty-one. PLT twenty-eight, even."
TRE: "Sixty-eight Profit sounds like I'm winning."
THE COLLECTOR: "Sixty-eight Profit with a sixty-one Tax is a man sprinting with his shoelaces tied together and calling it cardio."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 88 (—) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
TRE: P 68 (—) L 28 (—) T 58 (-3) │ PLT 29.2 ▲
*Analyst: Tre hears only the Profit line — the Collector makes him hear all three at once, and the Tax immediately loses its invisibility discount.*

**Beat 2 — PROFIT WITHOUT VALUE**
TRE: "I move product, I get paid, that's the whole job description."
THE COLLECTOR: "That's half the job description. The other half is value — and the corner hasn't seen yours since you set up."
TRE: "Value is for people with storefronts."
THE COLLECTOR: "You shorted Miss Odessa's change twice. You charge the kids to watch their own bikes. Last Tuesday you sold Dee an umbrella in the rain at triple — Dee, Tre. The woman who feeds this entire zip code."
TRE: "She paid it, though."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit taken without value isn't hustle. It's extraction. And extraction is just a Tax you charge everybody else — which is why yours is sixty-one and climbing in red ink."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 88 (—) T 21 (—) │ PLT 69.2 ▲
TRE: P 65 (-3) L 28 (—) T 58 (—) │ PLT 28.0 ▼
*Analyst: Put plainly, every no-value sale was booked against him — the Profit he bragged on gets marked down the second its source is read aloud.*

**Beat 3 — THE CORNER KEEPS RECEIPTS**
TRE: "Nobody ever said nothing to me about none of that."
THE COLLECTOR: "The corner keeps receipts even when people keep quiet. Page four: you carried Elias's groceries in March and wouldn't take the dollar."
TRE: "That was — he was gonna drop the bag. That's not value, that's reflex."
THE COLLECTOR: "Page five: you walk the Carter girl past the lot dogs every Thursday. Page six: you fix Dee's gate and tell her the wind did it."
TRE: "Why the wind gotta snitch, man."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book don't blink, Tre. It writes the good down too."
THE COLLECTOR: "Because even your good deeds know your branding is bad. You DO put value in, Tre — you just refuse to claim it, 'cause claiming it means the corner can hold you to it."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 89 (+1) T 21 (—) │ PLT 69.6 ▲
TRE: P 65 (—) L 32 (+4) T 53 (-5) │ PLT 31.6 ▲
*Analyst: The receipts cut both ways — the same book that lists his extractions lists his unclaimed value, and claiming it raises Love and cuts Tax in one move.*

**Beat 4 — SMALL TAX**
THE COLLECTOR: "So here's the small tax you pay starting today, before we do the big one. Shorted change goes back. Doubled."
TRE: "Doubled is crazy—"
THE COLLECTOR: "Shorting a grandmother is crazy. Doubled is arithmetic with a conscience. Kids' bikes get watched free — call it advertising. And Dee's umbrella money comes back with an apology stapled to it."
TRE: "You want me to work the corner like a charity?"
THE COLLECTOR: "I want you to work it like an owner. Owners feed the corner that feeds them. Renters strip it and move. Which one has a future on this block, runner?"
TRE: "...Owner money is slower."
THE COLLECTOR: "Owner money is LONGER. Slower is just longer wearing a watch."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 89 (—) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
TRE: P 66 (+1) L 36 (+4) T 53 (—) │ PLT 33.6 ▲
*Analyst: The Small Tax is behavioral, not financial — repay, un-charge, apologize — and each repayment is booked as Love going back into the corner.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Now the settlement, plain. The debt is profit taken without value — every dollar that left this corner lighter than it found it. The Agency collects that debt today. What you owe going forward is value first, price second."
TRE: "And if I just pay it and go back to how I was?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Then I'll see you next quarter, and the Tax will have brought friends. But you won't. I can read, Tre — your book already turned the page."
TRE: "Value first. Price second. ...Change goes back doubled, starting with Miss Odessa, so she can tell the whole block before I lose my nerve."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: "That's the smartest trade you ever made on this corner. Your receipt — Listen, tagged and true, a real Betty Plush soul. Big ears. Hears everything the corner isn't saying. Take notes."
TRE: "Tell the book I said I'll see it at a better number."
THE COLLECTOR: "It heard you. It's already writing."
THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+1) L 91 (+2) T 20 (—) │ PLT 71.6 ▲
TRE: P 67 (+1) L 48 (+12) T 30 (-23) │ PLT 48.0 ▲
*Analyst: The Settlement lands as the chapter's cleanest Tax collapse — twenty-three points — because the debt was never money; it was the value he finally agreed to put back.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 71.6 · P 73 · L 91 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
TRE — PLT 48.0 · P 67 · L 48 · T 30 — ◆◆ "VALUE GOES BACK IN"

**THE MOVE — "THE SURVIVAL PASS"**
*Tre's play: taking profit off the corner while calling it survival, so nobody felt they could audit him. The maxim: "Slap the word survival on a taking and asking for the receipt suddenly looks like the crime." The Collector's answer: READ ALL THREE LINES — the Reading reads Profit, Love and Tax together, lists the extractions and the unclaimed value, and the Settlement books value first, price second.*

**Takeaway:** *"Profit without value is just a tax you charge the block."*

**Who's in the Room:** THE COLLECTOR, soul collector for the Soul Collection Agency, pink book open on Tre's corner · TRE, young runner who takes profit off the corner and puts nothing back
**Beats:** 1. THE READING — the Collector reads Tre's high-Profit, high-Tax holding aloud 2. PROFIT WITHOUT VALUE — she lists the extractions: shorted change, charged kids, triple-priced umbrella 3. THE CORNER KEEPS RECEIPTS — his unclaimed good deeds are in the book too 4. SMALL TAX — change repaid doubled, bikes watched free, Dee refunded with an apology 5. THE SETTLEMENT — the no-value debt is collected; Listen is handed over as his receipt
**On-Screen Words:**
YOUR BOOK SAYS P SIXTY-EIGHT
PROFIT TAKEN WITHOUT VALUE
THE CORNER KEEPS RECEIPTS
OWNER MONEY IS LONGER
VALUE FIRST. PRICE SECOND.
**Score After:** THE COLLECTOR PLT 71.6 ◆◆◆◆ "RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER" · TRE PLT 48.0 ◆◆ "VALUE GOES BACK IN"
**The Move:** "THE SURVIVAL PASS" — *"Slap the word survival on a taking and asking for the receipt suddenly looks like the crime."* The Collector answers with READ ALL THREE LINES.
**Takeaway:** *"Profit without value is just a tax you charge the block."*
**Explainer Line (DRAFT — Pope's slot):** DRAFT: Tre thought a 68 Profit meant he was winning — until the Collector showed him the 61 Tax he was charging everybody else.

### TC1.3 — The Porch Light

*Dusk, and Miss Odessa's porch light is already on — it is always on: the Collector climbs the steps in her green-and-pink polka-dot fit, gold hoops, pink-and-blue hair catching the porch glow, ornate scythe hooked politely by the door like a guest's umbrella, Drippy the pink drip bunny plush in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book in hand — across the street the laundromat window glows BETTY PLUSH · 222 · PLT, and a hand-lettered AGENCY VISIT TONIGHT sign leans in Odessa's own window, because she knew.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · MISS ODESSA (owes three apologies on one block, pays interest in silence)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
MISS ODESSA — P 52 · L 61 · T 67 │ PLT 32.4

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
MISS ODESSA: "I put the sign up myself. If the Agency's coming to my porch, the neighbors might as well get a show with their supper."
THE COLLECTOR: "Miss Odessa, you are the only client who ever catered her own collection."
MISS ODESSA: "Read it, baby. I've been rehearsing my face."
THE COLLECTOR: "Your book says P fifty-two, L sixty-one, T sixty-seven. PLT thirty-two point four."
MISS ODESSA: "Sixty-seven Tax. Lord. That's not a number, that's a houseguest."
THE COLLECTOR: "It's three houseguests, actually. And they've been sleeping in your chest for years."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
MISS ODESSA: P 52 (—) L 61 (—) T 64 (-3) │ PLT 33.6 ▲
*Analyst: Odessa meets the Reading with humor, but the Tax still flinches — being seen is the first payment, and it always clears.*

**Beat 2 — THREE ENVELOPES NEVER SENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Three apologies, one block. Your sister June, for the will reading — what you said about her husband. Dee, for missing the repast and letting her think it was the casserole. And Elias, for laughing at the cookout. That laugh, Miss Odessa. Yours carried."
MISS ODESSA: "I wrote the letters. All three. They're in the flour tin, under the good sugar."
THE COLLECTOR: "Three envelopes never sent are three loans never settled. An apology written and withheld is Love in an envelope, baby — it earns you nothing in a tin."
THE COLLECTOR: "Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."
MISS ODESSA: "I was waiting for the right moment."
THE COLLECTOR: "The right moment sent you three reminders and then sent ME. I am what the fourth reminder looks like."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 69.2 ▲
MISS ODESSA: P 52 (—) L 58 (-3) T 64 (—) │ PLT 32.4 ▼
*Analyst: Confronting the withheld letters dips her Love line first — that's the cost of admitting the apologies existed all along and still weren't delivered.*

**Beat 3 — INTEREST IN SILENCE**
THE COLLECTOR: "Now the part nobody told you. An unpaid apology doesn't sit still. It charges interest, and the interest is silence. June calls less. Dee knocks on Elias's door twice so she doesn't have to knock on yours once. You hear how quiet your porch got?"
MISS ODESSA: "I told myself quiet was peace."
THE COLLECTOR: "Peace has visitors, Miss Odessa. That quiet is collections calling and you letting it ring. You've been paying interest in silence for years on debts that cost one sentence each."
MISS ODESSA: "One sentence. 'I was wrong, and I laughed, and I'm sorry.' That's the whole mortgage?"
THE COLLECTOR: "That's the principal. The silence was the interest — and baby, you have overpaid."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 90 (+1) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
MISS ODESSA: P 54 (+2) L 58 (—) T 59 (-5) │ PLT 35.2 ▲
*Analyst: Reframing her silence as interest payments — not peace — moves the number: she realizes she was never avoiding the cost, just paying the most expensive version of it.*

**Beat 4 — THE FIRST NAME**
THE COLLECTOR: "Order matters. Who gets the first apology?"
MISS ODESSA: "Elias. He's closest, and he's the one I laugh about in my sleep. June will need a chair and a beverage. Dee — Dee will forgive me before I finish the sentence, which somehow makes hers the hardest."
THE COLLECTOR: "Say the first name like you mean to walk toward it."
MISS ODESSA: "Elias. Tomorrow. On his stoop, with my own two feet and my own loud mouth, apologizing at the exact volume I laughed."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book just underlined that. In pink. It underlines in pink when it's proud."
MISS ODESSA: "Then tell it to keep the pen out. We got two more names coming."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 90 (—) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
MISS ODESSA: P 54 (—) L 66 (+8) T 59 (—) │ PLT 38.4 ▲
*Analyst: Naming the first recipient out loud is a Love deposit — intention spoken at full volume books immediately, before a single step is walked.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Then settle it plain. The debt is the apologies themselves — three of them, June, Dee, and Elias — and the Agency collects the promise today and the deliveries this week. The silence stops accruing tonight. Your porch light stays on for visitors again, not for warning."
MISS ODESSA: "Collected and scheduled. June gets a chair and the truth. Dee gets hers before the sentence ends. Elias gets the loud one, tomorrow, in public — where the crime occurred, as they say on television."
THE COLLECTOR: "Sixty-seven Tax, and most of it just left through the front door. Feel it?"
MISS ODESSA: "My chest sounds different. There's an echo where the houseguests were."
THE COLLECTOR: "That echo is room for company. Your receipt — Bell Paws, tagged and true, a real Betty Plush soul. Little bells, so from now on, when you arrive, people hear something sweet coming — not silence."
MISS ODESSA: "Stay for tea, Collector. First visitor under the new management."
THE COLLECTOR: "Miss Odessa, I thought you'd never redeposit."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+2) L 92 (+2) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.4 ▲
MISS ODESSA: P 55 (+1) L 76 (+10) T 37 (-22) │ PLT 51.6 ▲
*Analyst: The Settlement drops her Tax twenty-two points — the three apologies, once promised on the record, stop charging interest, and her Love line jumps as the deliveries are scheduled.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.4 · P 74 · L 92 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "THE BOOK UNDERLINES IN PINK"
MISS ODESSA — PLT 51.6 · P 55 · L 76 · T 37 — ◆◆ "INTEREST PAID IN FULL"

**THE MOVE — "THE SEALED ENVELOPE"**
*Miss Odessa's play: withholding three apologies to keep the upper hand, so her silence stayed both the punishment and the throne. The maxim: "A sorry locked away still sits at the head of the table, collecting silence from everyone waiting on it." The Collector's answer: DELIVER IT AT FULL VOLUME — the Reading prices the withheld apologies as interest paid in silence, and the Settlement collects the promise and schedules all three deliveries, starting with Elias at the volume she laughed.*

**Takeaway:** *"An apology withheld charges interest in silence."*

### TC1.4 — The One Who Carries the Block

*Evening, Dee mid-errand with two grocery bags, somebody's keys, and a child on each side who is not hers: the Collector falls into step in her green-and-pink polka-dot fit, gold hoops, pink-and-blue hair, ornate scythe over one shoulder, Drippy the pink drip bunny plush in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book in hand — under the BETTY PLUSH 222 mural, PLT painted three storeys high.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · DEE (runs every errand, watches every kid, has never named what it costs her)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
DEE — P 38 · L 92 · T 71 │ PLT 37.6

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
DEE: "If you're collecting me, you gotta carry a bag. Agency rules."
THE COLLECTOR: "Deal." *(taking a grocery bag)* "Now walk slower. This appointment comes to you."
THE COLLECTOR: "Your book says P thirty-eight, L ninety-two, T seventy-one. PLT thirty-seven point six."
DEE: "Ninety-two Love. See? I'm fine."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book don't blink, Dee. It read the Tax too."
THE COLLECTOR: "Ninety-two Love with a seventy-one Tax is a woman carrying a whole block uphill and describing the view. Your Tax is the highest in this chapter — and you never charged anybody a dime."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
DEE: P 38 (—) L 92 (—) T 68 (-3) │ PLT 38.8 ▲
*Analyst: Dee's book shocks even the Collector — the highest Love and the highest Tax on the block in the same holding, because her Tax is a cost she pays for everyone else.*

**Beat 2 — LOVE WITHOUT A RECEIPT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Count it with me. Every errand. Every kid. The repast, Elias's silence, spare keys for four houses and a dog with anxiety."
DEE: "Biscuit. His anxiety has anxiety. Your point?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Love without a receipt turns into a tax. Every errand you never named became a charge against YOUR account — because a cost nobody names doesn't disappear, baby. It moves in."
DEE: "If I start naming costs, people will think I keep score."
THE COLLECTOR: "You don't keep score. You ARE the score — you're just the only one never allowed to read it."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 69.2 ▲
DEE: P 36 (-2) L 92 (—) T 68 (—) │ PLT 38.0 ▼
*Analyst: The Collector separates naming from scorekeeping — Dee's Profit dips as she admits the pattern out loud for the first time.*

**Beat 3 — THE COST SHE NEVER NAMED**
DEE: "You want a cost named? I haven't had a Saturday in two years. I fell asleep at my own birthday — at the table, in the cake's eyeline."
THE COLLECTOR: "The cost she never named, finally named. Say the big one, Dee. The one under the errands."
DEE: "...Nobody asks if I need anything. And I built it that way. I'm the block's day off, and I never get one."
THE COLLECTOR: "That's an unnamed Tax with your name forged on it — and you forged it, out of love, which is the saddest penmanship I ever read."
DEE: "So I just stop? The block falls down?"
THE COLLECTOR: "The block finds out it has legs. It's been leaning so long it thinks leaning is standing."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 90 (+1) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
DEE: P 40 (+4) L 92 (—) T 63 (-5) │ PLT 41.6 ▲
*Analyst: Naming the true cost — not the errands, but the invisibility underneath them — cuts her Tax and raises her Profit at once: her time is finally booked as value.*

**Beat 4 — THE BLOCK'S ACCOUNT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Now the part that makes this collection different. This debt isn't yours to pay."
DEE: "Come again?"
THE COLLECTOR: "The block's account. The Agency bills the block, Dee. Not you. Two evenings a week come back to you. Errands rotate. The keys get a roster. Elias himself signed — his first words in three summers were 'put me down for Tuesdays.'"
DEE: "Elias said that? Out loud? In the air?"
THE COLLECTOR: "The block owes her, and the block is paying. Your only job here is the hardest one you've got: receive it without apologizing."
DEE: "...I can practice on that bag. You're carrying it wrong, by the way. It goes on the hip."
THE COLLECTOR: "Noted. Under 'grace, still learning.'"
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 90 (—) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
DEE: P 44 (+4) L 94 (+2) T 63 (—) │ PLT 44.0 ▲
*Analyst: The collection reverses direction — the debt is charged to the block's account, and Dee's numbers rise simply from standing still and being owed out loud.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Then settle it plain. The debt is Dee's unnamed Tax — two years of Saturdays, a birthday slept through, a whole woman treated as weather. The Agency collects it FROM the block's account, in full: time returned, errands rotated, keys rostered, and one standing order — the block asks Dee what she needs, first, before it asks her for anything."
DEE: "And if I reach for a bag that isn't mine next week? Out of habit?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Then I will appear and carry it myself, while making eye contact. Feel that? That's seventy-one getting lighter."
DEE: "...It's quiet in my chest. Nobody's keys. Nobody's list. Just — mine."
THE COLLECTOR: "Yours. And your receipt — Nightwing Dawn, tagged and true, a real Betty Plush soul. Wings for the night shift, baby — yours is officially over. Rest is a line item now."
DEE: "Walk me to my door. Slowly. I want the block to see me arriving empty-handed, like a celebrity."
THE COLLECTOR: "Empty-handed, owed, and on time for her own life."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+1) L 93 (+3) T 19 (-2) │ PLT 72.8 ▲
DEE: P 52 (+8) L 98 (+4) T 38 (-25) │ PLT 58.8 ▲
*Analyst: The Settlement is the chapter's biggest move — a twenty-five-point Tax drop, charged to the block, not to her — because the debt was never hers; collecting it from the right account sets the whole ledger straight.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.8 · P 73 · L 93 · T 19 — ◆◆◆◆ "SHE COLLECTS FROM THE RIGHT ACCOUNT"
DEE — PLT 58.8 · P 52 · L 98 · T 38 — ◆◆◆ "THE BLOCK OWES HER"

**THE MOVE — "THE CARRIER'S ARMOUR"**
*Dee's play: carrying the whole block so completely that nobody could refuse her anything and nobody dared ask what it cost her, making indispensability her armour. The maxim: "Become the one nobody can spare, and nobody will dare ask what being spared is costing you." The Collector's answer: BILL THE BLOCK, NOT THE CARRIER — the Reading names the cost Dee never named, and the Settlement collects her unnamed Tax from the block's account, returning her time and rotating the load.*

**Takeaway:** *"A cost nobody names doesn't disappear — it moves in."*

## CHAPTER TWO — The Corner Store & The Block

### TC2.1 — The Store Credit Book

*Han's Corner Store at opening time, BETTY PLUSH 222 signage over the door, real Betty Plush souls as tagged products in the window: THE COLLECTOR in a pink Betty Plush 222 tracksuit and polka-dot scarf, chunky sneakers, ornate scythe across her back, Drippy in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book open at the counter, PLT on the cover.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · MR. HAN (store owner, keeper of the block's credit book)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
MR. HAN — P 45 · L 60 · T 38 │ PLT 40.8

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
MR. HAN: "We open at eight. Whatever you're selling, I am not buying before coffee."
THE COLLECTOR: "I'm not selling, Mr. Han. I'm reading. Your page, out loud, since it's the one page in this book you've never read."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit forty-five. Love sixty. Tax thirty-eight. PLT forty point eight."
THE COLLECTOR: "You did. Then you charged them a Tax for it and never wrote it down. An unnamed Tax doesn't disappear, Mr. Han. It collects interest in silence."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
MR. HAN: P 45 (—) L 58 (-2) T 41 (+3) │ PLT 38.8 ▼
*Analyst: The Reading names the gap between Han's generosity and the resentment accruing underneath it — the Tax rises the moment it is seen.*

**Beat 2 — THE LEDGER BEHIND THE COUNTER**
MR. HAN: "That book under the counter is credit. Names, dates, what they owe. Clean system."
THE COLLECTOR: "Clean? Mrs. Okafor owes you eleven dollars from March and has apologised to you forty times. Eleven dollars, forty apologies. Your exchange rate is terrible."
THE COLLECTOR: "You tell her it's fine, but you never cross her name out. So it isn't fine. It's filed."
THE COLLECTOR: "If you never cross them out, people forget what you cost. There's a difference between a debt and a leash: whether it can ever be paid."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 69.6 ▲
MR. HAN: P 47 (+2) L 58 (—) T 39 (-2) │ PLT 40.4 ▲
*Analyst: Han has confused keeping the record with keeping the people.*

**Beat 3 — NO REPAYMENTS ACCEPTED**
THE COLLECTOR: "Tuesday. DeShawn came in with cash to settle his page. What did you say?"
MR. HAN: "I said... pay me when you're really on your feet."
THE COLLECTOR: "He was on his feet. He was standing right there, holding money, on his feet."
MR. HAN: "If they pay me back, they stop coming in every day. A paid-up customer is a stranger with good credit."
THE COLLECTOR: "There it is. You don't refuse repayment because you're kind. You refuse it because being owed is the only invitation you trust."
MR. HAN: "A man my age does not say 'please visit me' out loud."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 90 (+1) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
MR. HAN: P 47 (—) L 62 (+4) T 36 (-3) │ PLT 43.2 ▲
*Analyst: Han lets the block owe him but lets nobody repay, because an open debt guarantees company.*

**Beat 4 — OWED OUT LOUD**
MR. HAN: "So what, I forgive everybody? Burn the book? That's a sermon, not a business."
THE COLLECTOR: "Nobody's burning anything. You're going to do something harder. You're going to be owed out loud."
MR. HAN: "Meaning?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Meaning you say the true sentence. 'I like that you come.' Let people owe you affection in the open, where it can be repaid in the open — with visits, not apologies."
MR. HAN: "And the money in the book?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Collect it. Properly. With receipts and thank-yous and names crossed out in fat marker where everybody can watch. A paid debt should sound like a bell, not a secret."
THE COLLECTOR: "The scythe is decorative, Mr. Han. The arithmetic isn't."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 90 (—) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 70.8 ▲
MR. HAN: P 50 (+3) L 64 (+2) T 32 (-4) │ PLT 46.8 ▲
*Analyst: Openly acknowledged Love lets repaid Profit stop carrying a hidden Tax.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement, Mr. Han. The debt is not the block's loose change. It is your refusal to be owed out loud — forty years of kindness with the receipt withheld. That Tax is named now, and named Taxes get collected. Today."
THE COLLECTOR: "Pay it, and your book becomes a book again instead of a fence. Read your new page."
MR. HAN: "Profit fifty-five. Love seventy-two. Tax eighteen. PLT fifty-seven point six. I went UP for letting go?"
THE COLLECTOR: "You went up for finishing the transactions. Love that completes is worth more than love that lingers. That's the whole science."
MR. HAN: "Then take the book. No — wait. Give it back. I have names to cross out. Loudly."
THE COLLECTOR: "That's the spirit. And this is Mendie — a real Betty Plush soul, tag on, name on the label. Your receipt. Put her by the register, where the apologies used to go."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+2) L 92 (+2) T 19 (-1) │ PLT 72.8 ▲
MR. HAN: P 55 (+5) L 72 (+8) T 18 (-14) │ PLT 57.6 ▲
*Analyst: The Settlement collects the refusal itself: once Han can be owed openly, his Tax collapses and repaid debts pay him back in Love, not silence.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.8 · P 74 · L 92 · T 19 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
MR. HAN — PLT 57.6 · P 55 · L 72 · T 18 — ◆◆◆ "OWED OUT LOUD AT LAST"

**THE MOVE — "THE LEDGER THAT NEVER CLOSES"**
*Mr. Han's play: He keeps the whole block's small debts open in his credit book and refuses repayment out loud, so gratitude stays owed to him forever. The maxim: "A debt you refuse to finish will finish every friendship first." The Collector's answer: THE LOUD CROSS-OUT — the Reading names the withheld receipt as the Tax, and the Settlement makes him collect, receipt, and cross out names in the open, where affection can be repaid in visits instead of apologies.*

**Takeaway:** *"A debt that can never be repaid isn't kindness — it's a leash with good manners."*

**Beats:** 1. THE READING — She reads Han's own page aloud: P 45, L 60, T 38, PLT 40.8. 2. THE LEDGER BEHIND THE COUNTER — Clean Profit, unfinished Love; eleven dollars bought forty apologies. 3. NO REPAYMENTS ACCEPTED — He refuses repayment because a paid-up customer might stop coming. 4. OWED OUT LOUD — Be owed affection openly; cross out paid names loudly, like a bell. 5. THE SETTLEMENT — The refusal is named and collected; his Tax crashes to 18, and he takes Mendie as his receipt.

### TC2.2 — The Tab He Pretends Not to Know

*Saturday morning outside Han's Corner Store, BETTY PLUSH 222 banner over the awning, real Betty Plush souls as tagged products in the window: THE COLLECTOR in a pink Betty Plush 222 tracksuit and polka-dot scarf, chunky sneakers, ornate scythe across her back, Drippy in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book in hand, PLT down the spine.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · BIG ROME (Saturday regular, performer of strategic amnesia)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
BIG ROME — P 58 · L 52 · T 44 │ PLT 40.4

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
BIG ROME: "Morning, morning. Beautiful day. Whoever you are, I don't owe you and we've never met."
THE COLLECTOR: "Impressive. You denied a debt before I presented one. Let's see why. Opening your page, Big Rome."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit fifty-eight. Love fifty-two. Tax forty-four. PLT forty point four."
BIG ROME: "Tax forty-four? For WHAT? I'm one of the most generous men on this block. Ask anybody."
THE COLLECTOR: "I did. Everybody described the same man: generous with speeches, mysterious with tabs. Your Tax isn't spending, Rome. It's theatre. Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."
THE COLLECTOR: "Theatre is never free, Rome. Somebody always pays the admission — on this block it's been Han, every Saturday, smiling."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 88 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.4 ─
BIG ROME: P 58 (—) L 50 (-2) T 47 (+3) │ PLT 38.4 ▼
*Analyst: Rome's Profit and Love are decent — an unnamed Tax is eating both.*

**Beat 2 — SATURDAY AMNESIA**
THE COLLECTOR: "Last Saturday. Plantains, eggs, and that juice you pretend is for your nephew."
BIG ROME: "The juice is mine. Continue."
THE COLLECTOR: "Han says, 'Rome, that's nineteen on the tab with the rest.' And you say—"
BIG ROME: "I say, 'Tab? What tab? Han, you keep books on ME? I'm hurt.' It's a bit. Everybody loves the bit."
THE COLLECTOR: "Han laughs. Then he writes it down in pencil, like a man apologising to his own ledger. Your bit costs him twice: the money, and the dignity of asking."
THE COLLECTOR: "And you own a tab you pretend not to know about. One of those two facts is embarrassing, and it isn't his."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 88 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
BIG ROME: P 59 (+1) L 52 (+2) T 44 (-3) │ PLT 40.8 ▲
*Analyst: Rome performs ignorance so someone else carries the knowledge — and the cost — of his debt.*

**Beat 3 — THE LIE COSTS MORE**
BIG ROME: "Fine. Say I know about the tab. Say I've always known. It's still just money. Money's small."
THE COLLECTOR: "The money is small. The lie is not. The lie costs more than the groceries, Rome — it costs everyone watching a little faith that words mean things."
THE COLLECTOR: "There is. It's called Tax, it's forty-seven and falling: every 'what tab?' withdraws from the block's trust, and you have never made a deposit."
THE COLLECTOR: "True — your Love and Profit are real. That's why this is a collection, not a funeral. But a court that never pays rent is squatters with charisma."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 89 (+1) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 69.6 ▲
BIG ROME: P 60 (+1) L 55 (+3) T 40 (-4) │ PLT 44.0 ▲
*Analyst: The debt was never the groceries — it is the recurring lie that makes everyone else pretend along.*

**Beat 4 — EVERYBODY KNOWS**
BIG ROME: "If I walk in Saturday and just pay it, no bit — people will think I'm DYING."
THE COLLECTOR: "They'll think you're honest. Disorienting for everyone, briefly, like an eclipse."
BIG ROME: "And Han? Han LIKES the bit. Ask him."
THE COLLECTOR: "I collected Han this morning. His page is settled and he is crossing out names like a man ringing a bell. Your tab is the last pencil entry in that book, Rome. Everybody knows about the tab. The only person still performing not knowing is you."
BIG ROME: "Everybody knows. Even Ms. Patti?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Ms. Patti could recite your balance with your order. Plantains, eggs, juice for the nephew who does not drink juice. Okay?"
BIG ROME: "...Okay. OKAY."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 90 (+1) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
BIG ROME: P 63 (+3) L 58 (+3) T 35 (-5) │ PLT 48.4 ▲
*Analyst: Once the pretence is all that's left, dropping it becomes relief — Rome's Love jumps as the audience is released.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement. The debt is not the money, Big Rome. It's the lie — every Saturday of 'what tab?' stacked like unpaid rent on the truth. That Tax ends now. The money you'll pay Han yourself, in cash, out loud. Mine is the number."
BIG ROME: "Read it. Read my new page. Loud. I want witnesses."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit sixty-eight. Love seventy. Tax twenty. PLT sixty-one point two."
BIG ROME: "Sixty-one! From forty! Honesty pays BETTER than the bit? Why did nobody tell me honesty was lucrative?"
THE COLLECTOR: "I'm telling you. It's my whole job. And this is Pixel Heart — a real Betty Plush soul, tag on, name on the label. Your receipt. When the bit tempts you, look at her. She has never once pretended not to know anything."
THE COLLECTOR: P 75 (+3) L 92 (+2) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.8 ▲
BIG ROME: P 68 (+5) L 70 (+12) T 20 (-15) │ PLT 61.2 ▲
*Analyst: The Settlement collects the lie, not the money: Rome's Tax collapses and his real generosity finally registers as Love.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.8 · P 75 · L 92 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
BIG ROME — PLT 61.2 · P 68 · L 70 · T 20 — ◆◆◆ "PAID IN PUBLIC, PROUD OF IT"

**THE MOVE — "THE POLITE AMNESIA ACT"**
*Big Rome's play: Every Saturday he performs forgetfulness about his store tab, acting like there is no bill so the bill has to stay polite and Han has to carry it. The maxim: "A bill you act blind to still eats at somebody else's table." The Collector's answer: EVERYBODY KNOWS, OUT LOUD — the Reading prices the theatre as Tax, and the Settlement collects the lie itself, sending Rome to pay Han in cash, in public, with witnesses.*

**Takeaway:** *"The money was never the debt. The lie was — and a lie charges interest every single week."*

### TC2.3 — The Price Nobody Posted

*Midday at the register, BETTY PLUSH 222 price cards on the shelves, real Betty Plush souls as tagged products in the window behind the till: THE COLLECTOR in a pink Betty Plush 222 tracksuit and polka-dot scarf, chunky sneakers, ornate scythe across her back, Drippy in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book beside the scanner, PLT on the cover.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · MS. PATTI (cashier, quiet payer of other people's differences)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
MS. PATTI — P 42 · L 92 · T 48 │ PLT 48.4

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
MS. PATTI: "Next in line, baby. That means you, crown-and-scythe. You buying or browsing eternity?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Reading, Ms. Patti. Yours. Ring me up for the truth — nobody else here ever has."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit forty-two. Love ninety-two. Tax forty-eight. PLT forty-eight point four."
MS. PATTI: "Love ninety-two and Tax forty-eight? That math is disrespectful. I am the nicest person in this zip code."
THE COLLECTOR: "My book agrees with you. That's why the Tax is the story: a Tax that high on a Love that high means the kindness is real — and costing you a fortune."
MS. PATTI: "I agree to everything I pay. I'm a grown woman with a purse."
THE COLLECTOR: "A purse that's lighter every shift, for reasons on no receipt in this store. Shall we?"
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
MS. PATTI: P 42 (—) L 90 (-2) T 51 (+3) │ PLT 46.4 ▼
*Analyst: Maximal Love taxed at nearly half — the signature of generosity never allowed to be seen.*

**Beat 2 — THE DIFFERENCE**
THE COLLECTOR: "Yesterday, Mr. Abernathy. Eggs, bread, medicine money in coins — and he was short."
MS. PATTI: "He was short four dollars and thirty cents. Register must've miscounted. Happens."
THE COLLECTOR: "The register did not miscount. Your left hand went into your apron, and the difference walked out in his bag with the eggs. You paid the difference. Again."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book don't blink."
MS. PATTI: "That man counts coins like they're orders. You think I'm letting him leave here SHORT over four dollars?"
THE COLLECTOR: "I am not here to stop you paying. Nobody at the Agency has ever collected a kindness. Watch your page, not my scythe."
MS. PATTI: "Then what exactly is the charge, Collector?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Patience. We itemise before we settle."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 69.6 ▲
MS. PATTI: P 44 (+2) L 91 (+1) T 48 (-3) │ PLT 48.8 ▲
*Analyst: The kindness itself is cleared of all charges — the investigation targets only the secrecy around it.*

**Beat 3 — GENEROSITY IN HIDING**
THE COLLECTOR: "Six years at this register. Eleven hundred quiet rescues. Just under five thousand dollars out of your apron. Witnesses: zero. Thank-yous received: zero. Because a thank-you would require somebody to know."
MS. PATTI: "The second somebody knows, it stops being a gift and starts being a PERFORMANCE. I am not doing theatre."
THE COLLECTOR: "Rome's theatre was a lie. Yours would be a fact. There's a difference, and you know it."
MS. PATTI: "If they know, they stop coming. Pride is a real currency on this block. An old man who can't pay full price in secret will pay full price in hunger instead."
THE COLLECTOR: "True — your secrecy started as protection. But protection never switched off becomes a hiding place, and lately you've hidden in it from being owed anything yourself. When did anyone last do for you?"
MS. PATTI: "...Next question."
THE COLLECTOR: "That silence IS the Tax, Ms. Patti. Forty-eight points of it."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 90 (+1) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
MS. PATTI: P 46 (+2) L 94 (+3) T 44 (-4) │ PLT 52.4 ▲
*Analyst: Unnamed generosity: refusing to ever be repaid — or even witnessed — withdraws Love from the giver's own account until the Tax rivals the gift.*

**Beat 4 — THE BLOCK OWES HER**
THE COLLECTOR: "More elegant. The elders stay protected — their names never leave this book. But the block gets a page."
THE COLLECTOR: "Now the store opens a second book: the Patti Fund, posted by the register — anybody can put a dollar in for the next person's difference. No names of receivers. Ever. But your name goes on the cover — because the block owes her, and a debt the block knows about is a debt the block can finally repay."
MS. PATTI: "People will put money in a jar for strangers because my name's on it?"
THE COLLECTOR: "This block has tried to thank you for six years without knowing what for. Give them somewhere to put it. And if the jar overflows, the difference gets paid by everybody — where it belonged all along."
THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+2) L 90 (—) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 71.2 ▲
MS. PATTI: P 50 (+4) L 95 (+1) T 38 (-6) │ PLT 56.8 ▲
*Analyst: The design protects the receivers' pride while ending the giver's invisibility — the block is booked to repay her.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement, Ms. Patti. Hear the charge precisely: I am not collecting your generosity. It stays. It compounds. I am collecting the secrecy — the Tax you paid alone in the dark for six years so nobody would ever owe you. That ends today. The block is booked. You are owed, out loud, starting now."
MS. PATTI: "Read my page, then. Apparently everybody's going to hear it anyway."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit fifty-eight. Love ninety-nine. Tax twenty-two. PLT sixty-eight point zero."
MS. PATTI: "Sixty-eight. From forty-eight. Twenty points richer for letting people KNOW? Rudest arithmetic I've ever heard, and I work a register."
THE COLLECTOR: "Love that circulates is worth more than love that hides. Take Ember Stripe — a real Betty Plush soul, tag on, name on the label. Your receipt. She goes on the shelf behind you, where the block can see her."
MS. PATTI: "Put her facing the door. If I'm being owed out loud, I want witnesses."
THE COLLECTOR: P 75 (+2) L 93 (+3) T 19 (-1) │ PLT 73.6 ▲
MS. PATTI: P 58 (+8) L 99 (+4) T 22 (-16) │ PLT 68.0 ▲
*Analyst: Only the secrecy is collected: with the block booked to repay her, Patti's generosity starts raising her own ledger.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 73.6 · P 75 · L 93 · T 19 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
MS. PATTI — PLT 68.0 · P 58 · L 99 · T 22 — ◆◆◆ "OWED OUT LOUD, AT LAST"

**THE MOVE — "THE HELP NOBODY WAS ALLOWED TO SEE"**
*Ms. Patti's play: She quietly pays the elders' differences from her own pocket and tells no one, which keeps her kindness pure and also makes her the one person the store cannot run without. The maxim: "A gift that won't be witnessed still leaves the giver standing alone at the till." The Collector's answer: THE BLOCK BOOKED TO REPAY HER — the Settlement collects only the secrecy, keeps the receivers unnamed, and opens the Patti Fund under her name so the block can finally repay her in the open.*

**Takeaway:** *"Kindness done in secret still counts — but secrecy charges the giver full price."*

### TC2.4 — The Shelf She Stocks at Night

*Night outside Han's Corner Store, shutters half-down, BETTY PLUSH 222 window light on the pavement, real Betty Plush souls as tagged products behind the glass: THE COLLECTOR in a pink Betty Plush 222 tracksuit and polka-dot scarf, chunky sneakers, ornate scythe across her back, Drippy in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book under the streetlight, PLT on the cover.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · LIL BIT (teenager, unpaid night shelf-stocker, "family" when labour is needed)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
LIL BIT — P 28 · L 74 · T 52 │ PLT 34.0

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
LIL BIT: "Store's closed. And if you're here about the shelves, I already did aisle two, so it wasn't me."
THE COLLECTOR: "I'm here about a page, Lil Bit. Yours. Nobody's ever read you your own numbers, and you're seventeen, so that's already a debt owed TO you. Listen."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit twenty-eight. Love seventy-four. Tax fifty-two. PLT thirty-four point zero."
LIL BIT: "Profit twenty-eight? I stock this whole store, every night. Where's the rest?"
THE COLLECTOR: "My book never loses anything. Your profit didn't vanish, baby. It was taken — a little every night, in a currency nobody named. That is what a Tax of fifty-two looks like on a girl who works for free."
LIL BIT: "I don't work for free. I work for family."
THE COLLECTOR: "Hold that sentence. We're going to weigh it."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 88 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.4 ─
LIL BIT: P 28 (—) L 72 (-2) T 55 (+3) │ PLT 32.0 ▼
*Analyst: A worker's ledger taxed into the floor: high Love, almost no Profit retained — value extracted without ever being named.*

**Beat 2 — FAMILY WHEN IT'S FREE**
THE COLLECTOR: "Explain the arrangement. Like I'm the labour board — which tonight, I am."
LIL BIT: "Uncle Ray — not my REAL uncle, he's married to my mama's cousin — manages the store nights. He says family doesn't clock family. I stock, I sweep, I do the delivery boxes. A favour economy, he calls it."
THE COLLECTOR: "A favour economy. What favours have flowed back down it, Lil Bit? Itemise."
LIL BIT: "He lets me take the dented cans. And the bread nobody can sell. And he told my mama I'm 'learning responsibility.'"
THE COLLECTOR: "Five nights a week, and your wages are dented cans and a compliment delivered to your mother. That's not a favour economy. That's a one-way valve with a family discount on guilt."
LIL BIT: "If I ask for money, I'm ungrateful. That's the rule. Family doesn't ask."
THE COLLECTOR: "Notice who wrote that rule — and that it's only quoted when the free labour is flowing uphill."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 88 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
LIL BIT: P 30 (+2) L 74 (+2) T 51 (-4) │ PLT 35.2 ▲
*Analyst: 'Family' is used as a payment method — a word that converts wages owed into gratitude owed.*

**Beat 3 — PROFIT WITHOUT VALUE**
LIL BIT: "Say it IS messed up. What am I supposed to do, hand my uncle an invoice? At Sunday dinner?"
THE COLLECTOR: "You misunderstand the collection — happily, because this is the best part of my job. You are not the debtor on this page, Lil Bit. You're the address."
LIL BIT: "The address?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit taken without value given is the oldest unpaid Tax in the book. Ray takes your labour, books it as his management, pays you in dented cans. The debt is his. It travels through your page to reach him, the way a letter travels through an address. Profit without value. Say it — it's the charge."
LIL BIT: "Profit without value. ...It sounds like a charge when YOU say it. When I say it, it sounds like I'm about to get grounded by the entire bloodline."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 89 (+1) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 69.6 ▲
LIL BIT: P 34 (+4) L 76 (+2) T 46 (-5) │ PLT 39.6 ▲
*Analyst: The debt is reassigned to its true owner: she owes nothing — she is the channel through which the extraction is billed.*

**Beat 4 — HER PAGE, HIS DEBT**
THE COLLECTOR: "None of this requires you to fight a grown man at Sunday dinner. The Agency books Ray directly: two years of night shifts at the real rate, minus the dented cans at full retail — I am merciful, not soft."
LIL BIT: "Two YEARS of back pay? He'll say the store can't—"
THE COLLECTOR: "The store can. Han — settled this morning — never knew Ray staffed his store with an unpaid child and called it management. Han's book already has a line for a paid night stocker. Your uncle's discount was never store policy. It was his private arrangement with your silence."
LIL BIT: "And if Ray stops calling me family?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Then the word was a price tag, not a bloodline. Real family survives an invoice, baby."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 90 (+1) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
LIL BIT: P 38 (+4) L 79 (+3) T 40 (-6) │ PLT 44.8 ▲
*Analyst: Because Han was settled first, the remedy already exists — she never has to personally bill the man who exploited the word 'family'.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement. Let the record be plain: Lil Bit owes nothing. The debt collected tonight is profit taken without value — two years of a teenager's nights, taken by a man who called her family when he needed labour and never when labour needed paying. It is named on his page now, collected through hers, and her page is cleared first, in front of a witness. Me. Read your new numbers, stocker."
LIL BIT: "Profit fifty-two. Love eighty-six. Tax twenty-four. PLT fifty-nine point six. All that for... not owing anything?"
THE COLLECTOR: "For being OWED, finally, in writing. Your first paid shift starts tomorrow, at the posted rate, your name on the schedule. And this is Nightwing Dawn — a real Betty Plush soul, tag on, name on the label. Your receipt. She gets displayed in the window like she costs something. Because she does. Like you do."
LIL BIT: "Put her on aisle two. Let her watch me stock it once for money."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+2) L 92 (+2) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.4 ▲
LIL BIT: P 52 (+14) L 86 (+7) T 24 (-16) │ PLT 59.6 ▲
*Analyst: The Settlement collects the uncle's debt through her page and clears hers first: wages owed are booked as Profit restored, and the exploitative Tax collapses.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.4 · P 74 · L 92 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
LIL BIT — PLT 59.6 · P 52 · L 86 · T 24 — ◆◆◆ "OWED, IN WRITING, AT LAST"

**THE MOVE — "FAMILY AS A LOCKED DOOR"**
*Uncle Ray's play: Through Lil Bit's page, he calls her unpaid night labour "family," so the word does the locking and no wage may ever be discussed. The maxim: "Call the work kin and the wages never get a chair at the table." The Collector's answer: OWED IN WRITING — the Collector books the debt to Ray directly through her page, restores the back pay as her Profit, and puts her on the paid schedule by name.*

**Takeaway:** *"If 'family' only shows up when the work is free, it isn't family — it's a price tag wearing a bloodline."*

## CHAPTER THREE — The Boardroom

### TC3.1 — The Golden Ledger

*Forty-first floor, glass on three sides, a boardroom table long enough to land a plane on — THE COLLECTOR arrives in a tailored pink suit with a sharp blazer, black pumps, and a gold 222 brooch, her ornate scythe leaned on the boardroom table like it owns a seat, Drippy the pink drip bunny plush in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book in her hand, BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT cut into the frosted glass behind the head chair.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · GRANT HOLLOWAY (CEO, record profits, basement Love)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
GRANT HOLLOWAY — P 92 · L 28 · T 68 │ PLT 34.8

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "Miss, this is a closed board session. Whatever you're selling, my assistant—"
THE COLLECTOR: "I'm not selling, Grant. I'm collecting. Sit down, your book's already open."
THE COLLECTOR: "Grant Holloway. Profit ninety-two. Love twenty-eight. Tax sixty-eight. PLT thirty-four point eight."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "Thirty-four? We just posted the best quarter in company history."
THE COLLECTOR: "You posted the best Profit in company history. Love read the statement and moved out."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
GRANT HOLLOWAY: P 92 (—) L 28 (—) T 70 (+2) │ PLT 34.0 ▼
*Analyst: The Reading does no arguing — it just separates the three numbers he has spent years stapling together. The Tax rises the moment it is seen on paper; an unnamed Tax always bills for the looking.*

**Beat 2 — GOLDEN LEDGER**
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "Look at this ledger. Gold leaf. Audited. Every column reconciles to the cent."
THE COLLECTOR: "It's a beautiful book. Shame it's only got one column that anybody feeds."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "Profit is the column that keeps the lights on."
THE COLLECTOR: "Profit keeps the lights on. Love is why anybody stays in the room once they're lit."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "Sentiment doesn't show up in a quarterly report."
THE COLLECTOR: "It does, darling. It shows up as twenty-eight."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 69.6 ▲
GRANT HOLLOWAY: P 90 (-2) L 30 (+2) T 70 (—) │ PLT 34.0 ─
*Analyst: Golden ledger, golden frame, one fed column. She concedes his Profit is real — which costs him the argument that the other two numbers are fake.*

**Beat 3 — THE WORD EFFICIENCY**
THE COLLECTOR: "Page forty-one. Four hundred people leave in March. What did you call it?"
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "A restructuring. An efficiency measure."
THE COLLECTOR: "Efficiency. Such a clean word. It never has to learn anybody's name."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "If I'd called it what it felt like, the stock would have—"
THE COLLECTOR: "Fallen? Or felt it with you? Because right now only the four hundred did the feeling."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "I told myself the word was mercy. For the ones who stayed."
THE COLLECTOR: "A word that hides the cost isn't mercy, Grant. It's Tax with a tailor. Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 91 (+2) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
GRANT HOLLOWAY: P 88 (-2) L 35 (+5) T 66 (-4) │ PLT 36.8 ▲
*Analyst: This is the whole debt in one euphemism. Call a layoff "efficiency" and the Tax never gets a name, so it compounds in silence — naming it is the first payment.*

**Beat 4 — THE NAMES UNDER THE NUMBERS**
THE COLLECTOR: "Under your gold columns there's a second ledger. Want me to read it?"
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "There is no second ledger."
THE COLLECTOR: "There's always a second ledger. Yours is written in people who stopped bringing you problems before they got big."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "They stopped… escalating."
THE COLLECTOR: "They stopped trusting. Same silence, different book. Love doesn't quit loudly at your level, Grant. It just stops volunteering."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "And the Tax?"
THE COLLECTOR: "The Tax is every early warning you never got. You're flying a record-profit plane with the warning lights unscrewed."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 92 (+1) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 71.6 ▲
GRANT HOLLOWAY: P 85 (-3) L 42 (+7) T 58 (-8) │ PLT 41.6 ▲
*Analyst: Love withdrawn rarely announces itself in a boardroom; it shows up later as surprise — the resignation, the failure, the news nobody brought him early.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement time. The debt is named plainly: four hundred people, called efficiency, so the Tax never got a name."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "And if I name it now? Out loud, to the board?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Then it stops being Tax and starts being a cost you can actually pay. Say what it was. Fund the severance you trimmed. Read the number like a man who knows what it bought."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "Layoffs. They were layoffs. People, not efficiency."
THE COLLECTOR: "There it is. Paid."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: "Receipt, so you remember a ledger can start again — Pixel Heart, tagged and registered. Keep it where the gold book can see it."
GRANT HOLLOWAY: "My board is going to think I've lost my mind."
THE COLLECTOR: "No, darling. They're going to find out you've found it."
THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+2) L 94 (+2) T 19 (-1) │ PLT 73.6 ▲
GRANT HOLLOWAY: P 82 (-3) L 54 (+12) T 38 (-20) │ PLT 53.2 ▲
*Analyst: The biggest move lands where it always lands — not when Profit changes, but when the Tax is named out loud and paid in public. His Love jumps because naming the people is the first deposit back.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 73.6 · P 74 · L 94 · T 19 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
GRANT HOLLOWAY — PLT 53.2 · P 82 · L 54 · T 38 — ◆◆ "NAMED IT AT LAST"

**THE MOVE — "THE VELVET RENAME"**
*Grant Holloway's play: renaming four hundred layoffs "efficiency" so the cost was never read aloud in the room where it was decided. The maxim: "Dress a layoff in silk and four hundred families still eat the bare truth at their own tables." The Collector's answer: SAY IT PLAIN — she makes him say "layoffs, people, not efficiency" out loud to the board, so the Reading and the Settlement name the cost in the same words the families lived it.*

**Takeaway:** *"A layoff called efficiency is a Tax that never gets paid."*

**The Move:** "THE VELVET RENAME" — *"Dress a layoff in silk and four hundred families still eat the bare truth at their own tables."* The Collector answers with SAY IT PLAIN.
**Takeaway:** *"A layoff called efficiency is a Tax that never gets paid."*
**Explainer Line (DRAFT — Pope's slot):** DRAFT: He called four hundred layoffs "efficiency" — and an unnamed Tax is the kind that collects you.

### TC3.2 — The Layoff Memo

*An executive office two doors down from the boardroom, blinds half-drawn, a neat stack of unsigned memos squared to the desk's edge — THE COLLECTOR arrives in a tailored pink suit with a sharp blazer, black pumps, and a gold 222 brooch, her ornate scythe leaned on the boardroom table visible through the glass partition like it owns a seat, Drippy the pink drip bunny plush in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book in her hand, BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT etched down the glass in a line of frosted letters.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · SANDRA VOSS (VP, signer of eleven layoff memos)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
SANDRA VOSS — P 78 · L 34 · T 61 │ PLT 34.4

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
SANDRA VOSS: "I don't have this meeting in my calendar. I checked twice."
THE COLLECTOR: "You check everything twice, Sandra. Except the names. Sit down."
THE COLLECTOR: "Sandra Voss. Profit seventy-eight. Love thirty-four. Tax sixty-one. PLT thirty-four point four."
SANDRA VOSS: "That can't be right. My reviews are exemplary."
THE COLLECTOR: "Your reviews are. Reviews measure the signature. I measure what the signature cost. The book don't blink."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
SANDRA VOSS: P 78 (—) L 34 (—) T 63 (+2) │ PLT 33.6 ▼
*Analyst: Sandra's defence is process; the Reading uses her own standard against her — an accurate book, accurately read.*


**Beat 2 — ELEVEN MEMOS**
THE COLLECTOR: "Eleven layoff memos in six years. Your signature on every one."
SANDRA VOSS: "Eleven restructurings. HR drafted them. Legal cleared them. I executed a decision made above me."
THE COLLECTOR: "Above you, below you, drafted, cleared — everyone's hands are on the pen except yours, and yet there your name sits. In ink. Eleven times."
SANDRA VOSS: "Signing is not deciding."
THE COLLECTOR: "No. Signing is agreeing to be the face a stranger reads at the worst moment of their year. That's not nothing, Sandra. That's the whole job you actually did."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 69.6 ▲
SANDRA VOSS: P 76 (-2) L 36 (+2) T 63 (—) │ PLT 33.6 ─
*Analyst: "I just signed it" is how a Tax gets distributed until nobody owes it. She pins the debt to the one act Sandra actually owns: the signature.*

**Beat 3 — THE FIRST NAME**
THE COLLECTOR: "Memo four. Page two. Read me the first name on the list."
SANDRA VOSS: "I can't. I never— the lists were attached separately."
THE COLLECTOR: "The names were attached to the people too. That didn't stop anyone."
SANDRA VOSS: "I didn't read them on purpose. If I read them, I couldn't sign."
THE COLLECTOR: "I know. That's the most honest sentence in this office all year."
SANDRA VOSS: "…Marcus Webb. Nineteen years. He trained me on my first rotation."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 91 (+2) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.4 ▲
SANDRA VOSS: P 74 (-2) L 41 (+5) T 59 (-4) │ PLT 36.4 ▲
*Analyst: The debt was never eleven memos — it was eleven unread lists. One name read aloud does what six years of clean process couldn't: it makes the cost a person again.*

**Beat 4 — THE SIGNATURE**
SANDRA VOSS: "Do you know what happens to VPs who refuse to sign? They get a memo with their own name on it."
SANDRA VOSS: "So what was I supposed to do? Fall on my sword eleven times?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Read the names before you signed. Sit with one manager. Ask who could be moved instead of removed. Spend some Love before the Profit decision was final, not after."
SANDRA VOSS: "That would have slowed everything down."
THE COLLECTOR: "Yes. Slowing down is what Love looks like in a boardroom, Sandra. Speed is just Tax in a hurry."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 92 (+1) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 71.6 ▲
SANDRA VOSS: P 72 (-2) L 48 (+7) T 51 (-8) │ PLT 41.6 ▲
*Analyst: She refuses the villain edit and the martyr edit both. Sandra had room to spend Love inside the decision — time, questions, alternatives — and spent none of it.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement time. The debt is named plainly: the names. Eleven lists you signed and never read, so the Tax never got a name — it got three hundred and nine of them, and you carried every one unread."
SANDRA VOSS: "Three hundred and nine. You counted."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book counts. Now pay: read them. All of them, aloud, to yourself, tonight. It's not theatre. It's a deposit — the first one you've made in six years."
SANDRA VOSS: "Then it's paid?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Then it's named, and a named Tax can finally stop charging interest. Paid. Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: "Receipt — Bell Paws, tagged and registered. Soft paws, sharp memory. Keep it by the unsigned memos."
SANDRA VOSS: "The next memo… I'll read it first. Even if I still have to sign."
THE COLLECTOR: "Especially if you still have to sign."
THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+1) L 94 (+2) T 19 (-1) │ PLT 73.2 ▲
SANDRA VOSS: P 70 (-2) L 60 (+12) T 31 (-20) │ PLT 53.6 ▲
*Analyst: The biggest drop comes from an act with no audience and no upside: reading every name aloud. Love redeposited in private still counts on the book — the book always knew.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 73.2 · P 73 · L 94 · T 19 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
SANDRA VOSS — PLT 53.6 · P 70 · L 60 · T 31 — ◆◆ "READ THE NAMES"

**THE MOVE — "THE UNREAD SIGNATURE"**
*Sandra Voss's play: signing eleven layoff memos without ever reading a single name on them, so the dirt stayed on the paper and never reached her hands. The maxim: "A hand that signs without looking still leaves fingerprints on every life it moves." The Collector's answer: READ EVERY NAME — she sends her home to read all three hundred and nine names aloud, turning the Settlement into a roll call no signature can hide behind.*

**Takeaway:** *"A signature without a name read aloud is a Tax in your own handwriting."*
