## CHAPTER SEVEN — The Church Office

### TC7.1 — The Offering Ledger

*The church office on a Monday morning, count table cleared, offering plates stacked by the door. THE COLLECTOR in an elegant cream-and-gold fit with a matching headwrap, modest and fly, her ornate scythe planted respectfully by the office door with a cream ribbon tied to it, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book on the table beside a BETTY PLUSH 222 folder and a small PLT seal.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · DEACON WALTER PRICE (deacon, has counted the offering for thirty years)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
DEACON WALTER PRICE — P 55 · L 60 · T 48 │ PLT 40.8

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "Monday is count day. Nobody disturbs the count."
THE COLLECTOR: "Then I will be brief, Deacon, and respectful. I am not here for the money in the plates. I am here for a second book."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "There is one ledger. I keep it myself."
THE COLLECTOR: "You keep two. The first one balances. Listen to the second one, in your own opening holding. Walter Price: Profit fifty-five, Love sixty, Tax forty-eight. PLT forty point eight."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "That second number is not church business."
THE COLLECTOR: "No. It is yours. That is why I brought my book instead of theirs. Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+2) L 88 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 69.2 ▲
DEACON WALTER PRICE: P 55 (—) L 58 (-2) T 50 (+2) │ PLT 39.2 ▼
*Analyst: The Reading separates the official ledger from the private one. Walter's Tax rises the moment the hidden book is named, because unnamed is how it stayed free.*

**Beat 2 — THE SECOND BOOK**
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "I have never taken a dollar. Not one. Ask anybody."
THE COLLECTOR: "I believe you. Theft is not the line item, Deacon."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "Then what am I accused of?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Keeping score of the givers. Sister put in three dollars, so she gets a smaller greeting. A young man put in fifty, so his mistake gets a softer name. That is the second book. Names, amounts, and judgements, in pencil."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "Somebody has to notice who is serious."
THE COLLECTOR: "Noticing is a job. Judging was never assigned to you."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (—) L 90 (+2) T 22 (—) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
DEACON WALTER PRICE: P 52 (-3) L 58 (—) T 52 (+2) │ PLT 37.2 ▼
*Analyst: Walter defends his honesty about money to avoid his dishonesty about people. Profit falls because thirty years of trusted counting was spent, in part, on ranking.*

**Beat 3 — WHO ASSIGNED YOU?**
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "I only wrote what I saw. The envelope does not lie."
THE COLLECTOR: "The envelope tells you an amount. It does not tell you a rent payment missed, a medicine bought, a grandchild fed. You filled the silence with judgement."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "And if I had asked, they would have lied out of pride."
THE COLLECTOR: "Maybe. Pride is their tax to pay. You collected it anyway, in advance, every Monday."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "Who assigned you, then?"
THE COLLECTOR: "A fair question. The Soul Collection Agency. Betty Plush, two two two. My assignment is written down. Yours, for that second book, never was."
THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+2) L 90 (—) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 71.2 ▲
DEACON WALTER PRICE: P 52 (—) L 62 (+4) T 48 (-4) │ PLT 40.4 ▲
*Analyst: The Collector answers his challenge with paperwork of her own — assignment named, authority limited. Walter's Love rises when he finally hears the difference between his real duty and his invented one.*

**Beat 4 — THE COUNT HE MISSED**
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "Thirty years I counted. What did I miss?"
THE COLLECTOR: "The widow who gave two dollars and then stayed to mop this office. The boy who gave nothing and carried the plates so an old man's hands would not shake. Your first book counted both afternoons correctly: zero."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "Zero was accurate."
THE COLLECTOR: "Accurate and wrong. That is the most expensive kind of bookkeeping, Deacon."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "I cannot recount thirty years."
THE COLLECTOR: "You do not have to. You have to stop adding to the wrong column."
THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (—) L 92 (+2) T 21 (—) │ PLT 72.0 ▲
DEACON WALTER PRICE: P 55 (+3) L 68 (+6) T 40 (-8) │ PLT 47.2 ▲
*Analyst: This is the missed count: value given in time and care never entered either ledger. Naming it redeposits Love Walter had withdrawn from people he had already priced.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "The settlement is plain. The debt is a ledger of judgements you were never assigned to keep. Not the money — the judgements. I collect the book, Deacon. Not the man."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "And the counting? Do I lose the counting?"
THE COLLECTOR: "You keep the counting. You were always good at the counting. You lose the ranking. From now on, an envelope is an envelope, and a person is a person, and your pencil only touches one of them."
DEACON WALTER PRICE: "Then take it. Thirty years is heavy enough without carrying everybody's worth too."
THE COLLECTOR: "Collected. And here is your receipt — a small Betty Plush soul, Pixel Heart, with its tag on. When the pencil wants to write a judgement, hold that instead. A new start has to fit in the hand that does the work. Settled."
THE COLLECTOR: P 76 (+2) L 94 (+2) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 74.0 ▲
DEACON WALTER PRICE: P 58 (+3) L 75 (+7) T 22 (-18) │ PLT 58.4 ▲
*Analyst: The biggest move is the Tax drop: eighteen points of judgement, carried weekly for thirty years, finally named and paid. Walter leaves with his real job intact and his invented one collected.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 74.0 · P 76 · L 94 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "ASSIGNED AND ACCURATE"
DEACON WALTER PRICE — PLT 58.4 · P 58 · L 75 · T 22 — ◆◆◆ "COUNTS MONEY, NOT PEOPLE"

**THE MOVE — "THE PENCIL COURT"**
*Deacon Walter Price's play: For thirty years he kept a second, private ledger of who gave what — never stealing a cent, only quietly judging each giver by the amount and making himself the congregation's unappointed court. The maxim: "A private score kept on other people quietly becomes the sentence they never got to appeal." The Collector's answer: THE OPEN COUNT — The Collector names the hidden book out loud, leaves Walter the honest counting, and collects the ranking he was never assigned.*

**Takeaway:** *"An envelope is an envelope, and a person is a person — a clean book only ever counts one of them."*

**Beats:** 1. THE READING — The Collector reads Walter's opening holding and names the second, private book. 2. THE SECOND BOOK — Walter defends never stealing; she shows the theft was ranking givers by amount. 3. WHO ASSIGNED YOU? — She proves judging was never his assignment, while hers is written down. 4. THE COUNT HE MISSED — She recounts the care his ledgers recorded as zero. 5. THE SETTLEMENT — She collects the ledger of judgements, leaves him the counting, and hands him Pixel Heart as his receipt.

### TC7.2 — The Building Fund

*The church office on a Tuesday, a leak bucket in the corner catching a slow, polite drip from the ceiling. THE COLLECTOR in an elegant cream-and-gold fit with a matching headwrap, modest and fly, her ornate scythe planted respectfully by the office door with a cream ribbon tied to it, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book open beside a BETTY PLUSH 222 folder, a PLT seal, and the building fund binder, which is immaculate.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · TREASURER ODESSA GRANT (church treasurer, guardian of the building fund for ten years)

**OPENING HOLDING**

TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "Profit sixty-eight. You see? Somebody in this building understands stewardship."

**Beat 2 — THE TEN-YEAR GUARD**
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "Ten years I have guarded that fund. Through a flood year, a furnace year, and two pastors with expensive visions. It has never lost a dollar."
THE COLLECTOR: "It has never gained a building either. The fund is perfect, Odessa. The building is crumbling politely around it."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "Polite is better than foreclosed."
THE COLLECTOR: "The bucket in the corner has seniority now. It has been here longer than some members."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "One emergency and you will thank me."

**Beat 3 — SAFETY IS NOT A ROOF**
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "You make safety sound like a sin. Safety is love with a lock on it."
THE COLLECTOR: "Safety is love with a plan. A lock with no date on it is fear with good handwriting."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "And if I release it and the boiler goes the same winter?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Then you do what this congregation has always done. You tell the truth, you pass the plate, and people rise. You have trusted the fund for ten years, Odessa. You have not trusted the people once."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "That is a hard sentence."
THE COLLECTOR: "It is a soft one. I said it in an office, not in a sermon."

**Beat 4 — PROFIT PAST ITS PURPOSE**
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "The founders gave that money for this building. Spending it feels like breaking a promise."
THE COLLECTOR: "Keeping it while the building fails is the break, Odessa. They did not give it to a binder. They gave it to a roof, a nursery, a hall that does not rain indoors."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "Profit has a purpose. You sound like my training."
THE COLLECTOR: "Then hear the rest of the lesson. Profit kept past its purpose stops being profit. It becomes a tax the future pays to the past, with interest, in buckets."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "So the fund is not my faithfulness."
THE COLLECTOR: "Your faithfulness is real. It is just standing in the wrong account."

*Analyst: Core PLT doctrine, taught straight: Profit is only Profit while it moves toward its purpose. Held past that point, it converts — silently — into Tax.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "The settlement, plainly. The debt is love hoarded in the name of safety — profit kept past its purpose. I collect the hoarding, Odessa. The fund stays. The guarding ends."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "What does ending look like, in actual paperwork?"
THE COLLECTOR: "A release schedule, signed in this office. Roof this year, boiler reserve kept honest, and a line in every report that says what the money is for. Money with a mission, not a museum."
TREASURER ODESSA GRANT: "I can draft that. I draft everything."
THE COLLECTOR: "I know. That is why you will survive your own settlement. And your receipt — a small Betty Plush soul, Ember Stripe, tag on, bright as a work order. Put it on the binder. The next treasurer should know this fund once learned to move. Settled."

*Treasurer Odessa Grant's play: She guarded the building fund so tightly for ten years that safety itself became her throne, while the building it was meant to save crumbled politely around the untouched balance. The maxim: "Safety that never sets a date to serve becomes the master of the house it was hired to protect." ### TC7.3 — The Secretary's Book

*The church office on a Wednesday evening, prayer-request cards stacked in neat, heavy piles beside a desk lamp. CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "Love eighty-four. Well. Somebody finally wrote down the overtime."
THE COLLECTOR: "Love was never your problem. Look at the Tax standing next to it, almost as tall. That is not your tax, Miss Laverne. That is everybody else's, and you are carrying it home in your purse. Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "Somebody has to carry the prayer cards."
THE COLLECTOR: "Carry them to the office, yes. Carry them to sleep, no."

**Beat 2 — THE CARDS SHE CARRIES**
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "A card comes in, I type it, I file it. That is the job."
THE COLLECTOR: "The job ends at the filing cabinet. Tell me what happens at your kitchen table."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "I remember them. The diagnosis on Tuesday. The son in trouble on Thursday. In my dreams, if we are being exact."
THE COLLECTOR: "We are always exact. You know every family's business in this congregation, and you have told none of it. That discretion is gold, Miss Laverne. But discretion is not the same as storage."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "If I put it down, who picks it up?"
THE COLLECTOR: "That question is the lock on the whole book. We will come back to it."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+2) L 90 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: P 41 (-3) L 82 (—) T 53 (+2) │ PLT 42.0 ▼
*Analyst: Laverne's Profit dips because her real labor — the remembering — was never on any job description or any payroll. Unnamed work still bills the person doing it.*

**Beat 3 — CARRYING IS NOT CARING**
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "If I stop carrying it, I stop caring. That is how it feels from the inside."
THE COLLECTOR: "From the outside, it looks like this: the caring is yours, and it is beautiful. The carrying is a debt other people owe you, and nobody has been billed."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "You cannot bill a congregation for kindness."
THE COLLECTOR: "I am not billing them for kindness. I am booking them for their share. A sorrow carried by one woman is a private tax. The same sorrow carried by a whole room is called support. The weight does not change. The number of shoulders does."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "Shoulders. I have been one woman doing a room's work."
THE COLLECTOR: "And doing it so quietly that the room believes the work does itself."

CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: P 41 (—) L 86 (+4) T 47 (-6) │ PLT 46.0 ▲
*Analyst: The chapter's central distinction, stated plainly: the Collector collects the carrying, never the caring. Separating the two is what lets Laverne's Love rise instead of fall.*

**Beat 4 — PUT THE SORROW DOWN**
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "Put it down how? People trust me because I do not pass their business around like a casserole."
THE COLLECTOR: "And you will not start. No names leave this office — that rule stands, and I would collect anybody who broke it. But a card can be carried without a name. 'A family in this room needs meals this week.' The room can hold the weight without ever holding the secret."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "They would actually sign up. They ask me how to help and I say 'pray,' because the details are not mine to give."
THE COLLECTOR: "Then give them the shape, not the story. Put the sorrow down in this office, Miss Laverne, on that desk, where a whole congregation can reach it."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "And I go home with an empty purse."
THE COLLECTOR: "You go home with your own life in it. Imagine the room."

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "The settlement, and I will write it in your book myself. The debt is other people's sorrows, carried unnamed, nightly, alone. I collect the carrying, Miss Laverne. The caring stays right where it lives — with you, at full value."
CHURCH SECRETARY MISS LAVERNE COLE: "And the booking you mentioned?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Starting Sunday, the congregation carries you back. A standing line in the bulletin, in your own handwriting if you like: the secretary's list is a shared list. Rides, meals, visits — booked to the room, not to one woman's kitchen table."

THE COLLECTOR: "Then this one will be historic. Your receipt — a small Betty Plush soul, Bell Paws, tag on, soft enough to retire a heavy purse. When a sorrow tries to follow you home, hand it to Bell Paws until morning, and let the room do its shift. Settled."

*Church Secretary Miss Laverne Cole's play: She became the congregation's living memory, carrying every family's sorrows home from the prayer-request cards, until being the one who never put it down meant no one could ever ask her to rest. The maxim: "When one heart becomes the only place a whole room keeps its sorrows, remembering turns into a debt no single heart should pay alone." ### TC7.4 — The Tithe Envelope

*The church office on a Thursday, usher schedules pinned to a corkboard, a stack of small offering envelopes fanned on the desk. THE COLLECTOR in an elegant cream-and-gold fit with a matching headwrap, modest and fly, her ornate scythe planted respectfully by the office door with a cream ribbon tied to it, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book open, a BETTY PLUSH 222 folder and PLT seal beside the envelopes.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL (young usher, passes the offering plate every Sunday)

**OPENING HOLDING**

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "I just came to fix the usher schedule. I am not on any list."
THE COLLECTOR: "Jerome, everybody who passes the plate is on a list. The kind one. Sit down — gently, now, this is a gentle appointment. Your opening holding: Profit thirty-eight, Love sixty-two, Tax fifty-eight. PLT thirty point eight."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "Thirty point eight. That sounds about right for me in this building."
THE COLLECTOR: "It sounds like a good young man paying a tax nobody charged him. Love sixty-two is real, Jerome. So we start with the question your book is already asking: what is the fifty-eight?"
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "You would have to ask the plate. I pass it. I do not... I have not put anything in it in a while."
THE COLLECTOR: "A while. We will get the exact while from the book, not from your shame. Shame rounds up."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 88 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: P 38 (—) L 60 (-2) T 60 (+2) │ PLT 29.2 ▼
*Analyst: The gentlest Reading in the chapter: the Collector names his Love before his Tax and warns him his shame inflates its own numbers. She will use the book's figures, not his.*

**Beat 2 — THE EMPTY ENVELOPE**
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "Two years. It has been two years since my envelope went in. I hold the plate for everybody else's and mine stays in my pocket, empty, or at home, which is worse somehow."
THE COLLECTOR: "Why did it stop?"
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "Work got thin. What I could put in got small. Then I saw the man next to me put in an envelope fat enough to stand up, and mine felt like a receipt. So I skipped. Then it became my thing. The usher who does not give."
THE COLLECTOR: "And every Sunday since, you have paid for that one Sunday."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "Paid with what? That is the whole problem. I do not have it."
THE COLLECTOR: "Oh, you have paid plenty, Jerome. Just not in the envelope. The book don't blink."

YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: P 35 (-3) L 60 (—) T 62 (+2) │ PLT 27.2 ▼
*Analyst: The empty envelope was never empty of cost — Jerome has been paying weekly, in advance, in shame. His Profit falls here because he finally counts the two years honestly.*

**Beat 3 — SHAME IS NOT AN OFFERING**
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "So I owe two years of envelopes. Say the number. I would rather owe a number than feel like this."
THE COLLECTOR: "That is the trap talking. You want a big frightening number so you can keep hiding from a small honest one. Listen carefully, because this is the whole collection: shame is not an offering. Nobody's book accepts it. It buys nothing, builds nothing, feeds nobody. It just bills the person carrying it."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "Then what have I been paying?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Tax. Fifty-eight points of it, self-assessed, self-collected, every Sunday, in the exact seat where you were supposed to be free. You taxed yourself for being poor in a room that was built for poor people to be rich in."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "Built for... say that part again."
THE COLLECTOR: "Later. It will sound better when your number is clean."

*Analyst: Doctrine at its softest: a self-assessed Tax is still a Tax, and shame is a currency no ledger honors. Jerome's Love rises first — he believes, before anything changes, that the room was built for him.*

**Beat 4 — THE AMOUNT WAS NEVER THE LINE**
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "But the envelope matters. The roof, the lights — Deacon counts real money, not feelings."
THE COLLECTOR: "The envelope matters. The amount was never the line item. Read any honest ledger in this office: the line is 'given, freely, by name.' Yours has been blank for two years not because the amount was small, Jerome, but because shame told you small meant worthless, and worthless meant stay home from your own row."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "I usher. I am there every Sunday. Doors, plates, fans for the old ladies."
THE COLLECTOR: "Hours, every week, at full value — and you booked yourself at zero because the envelope was light. That is Walter's disease in a young man's body, and I just collected his book on Monday."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "So what goes in the envelope, when there is almost nothing to put?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Almost nothing, put in freely, outranks a fortune put in for show. That is the oldest arithmetic in this building, and it has never once been revised."

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "The settlement, and it is the gentlest one in my book this month. The debt is two years of tax paid on shame — not on money owed, Jerome, on shame felt. I collect the shame. All of it. The envelope was never the debt."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "And Sunday? What do I do Sunday?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Sunday you usher, same doors, same plate. And when the plate reaches you, your envelope goes in — with what you honestly have, which some weeks will be small and some weeks will be smaller, and every week it will count in full, because it was given by a free man and not extorted by a frightened one."
YOUNG USHER JEROME BELL: "And if it is two dollars?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Then it is two dollars at full value, which is more than two years of shame ever bought. Your receipt — a small Betty Plush soul, Mendie, tag on, patched and perfect. Mendie is for things that were mended, not replaced. Keep it by your usher badge. Settled."

**THE MOVE — "THE HIDDEN ENVELOPE"**

## CHAPTER EIGHT — The Group Chat & The Live

### TC8.1 — The Group Chat Debt

*Inside the group chat itself at 1:47 a.m. — forty-seven threads stacked like chairs, three condolence messages pinned under silence; THE COLLECTOR steps through the screen in neon streetwear, a cropped jacket with a glowing 222, her ornate scythe with a ring-light clipped to the shaft, phone in hand, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm photobombing every frame, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book open, BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding glowing in the chat header.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · TEE (admin of the family-and-friends group chat, reads everything, answers nothing hard)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

TEE — P 45 · L 30 · T 48 │ PLT 24.8

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

TEE: "How did you get in here? This chat is invite-only. I set it that way myself."

THE COLLECTOR: "You set everything to private, baby. That's why I'm here. The Collector, Soul Collection Agency — Betty Plush, 222, PLT: Profit, Love, Tax. Reading your page aloud. Profit forty-five. Love thirty. Tax forty-eight. PLT twenty-four point eight."

TEE: "That can't be right. I'm the admin. Nothing in this chat moves without me."

THE COLLECTOR: "Exactly. Nothing moves. You read every message within four minutes — blue ticks like fingerprints — and answered the hard ones with silence. Eleven replies owed, Tee. Three of them condolences."

TEE: "I didn't know what to say."

THE COLLECTOR: "You knew what to say for the sneaker drop. Six paragraphs for the sneaker drop."

THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲

TEE: P 45 (—) L 30 (—) T 50 (+2) │ PLT 24.0 ▼

*Analyst: The Reading names it — not absence, but selective presence.*

**Beat 2 — READ RECEIPT**

TEE: "Reading isn't a crime. Half the chat reads and doesn't answer."

THE COLLECTOR: "And your silence lands loudest, admin. Your cousin posted her mother was in surgery, thirty-one people saw your name under 'seen by' — then you posted a meme about parking. That's love withdrawn in read receipts, entered nightly."

TEE: "I was nervous, not gone."

THE COLLECTOR: "Nervous I believe. But people stopped telling this chat the true things. They tell you weather now — safe little weather reports to a man standing in their storm. There's a second chat, Tee. It's called 'The Real One.'"

TEE: "That's cold."

THE COLLECTOR: "That's accounting."

THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 69.2 ▲

TEE: P 44 (-1) L 32 (+2) T 50 (—) │ PLT 24.4 ▲

*Analyst: She refuses the admin alibi. Admitting his absence redirected the whole chat's honesty is his first small Love deposit in months.*

**Beat 3 — CONDOLENCE DEBT**

THE COLLECTOR: "Now the three. Mrs. Adeyemi's husband in March — you typed 'So sor' and deleted it. Marcus's brother in May — prayer hands, no words. Your own auntie in September — a thumbs-up on the funeral details, like it was a brunch plan."

TEE: "A thumbs-up meant I saw it!"

THE COLLECTOR: "It meant they saw you seeing it. A condolence isn't information. It's a deposit. 'I'm sorry. He mattered. I'm here.' Three sentences, no costume. You kept drafting the perfect reply until the moment passed, then called the silence respect."

TEE: "What if I say it wrong?"

THE COLLECTOR: "You already did — eleven times, in perfect silence. Wrong words can be corrected. Absent words compound."

THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 91 (+2) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.4 ▲

TEE: P 44 (—) L 38 (+6) T 46 (-4) │ PLT 28.4 ▲

*Analyst: Perfectionism is separated from care — grief requires presence, not eloquence.*

**Beat 4 — THE TYPING BUBBLE**

TEE: "Sometimes I type for ten minutes. The bubble shows, everybody waits, then I delete it because it sounds fake."

THE COLLECTOR: "The famous bubble. Three dots doing cardio, going nowhere. The chat has a sticker for it: Tee is performing concern again."

TEE: "Lord."

THE COLLECTOR: "Here's the rule of the chat and the rule of the book: presence is a streak, not an event. A short true sentence sent beats a beautiful sentence drafted. 'I read this twice. I don't have words, but I'm not going anywhere.' The Love column doesn't grade spelling, Tee. It grades arrival — your sister's scan results, your nephew's recital, all eight ordinary replies. Same medicine."

THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+1) L 91 (—) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.8 ▲

TEE: P 46 (+2) L 44 (+6) T 42 (-4) │ PLT 33.2 ▲

*Analyst: The typing bubble is exposed as concern performed at no cost and paid to nobody. A sent, imperfect sentence is reclassified as the actual currency.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**

THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement. Name it plain, admin."

TEE: "I owe the chat eleven replies. Three are condolences I ran from because I wanted to sound right instead of be there. My silence wasn't respect. It was love withdrawn in read receipts."

THE COLLECTOR: "Named, it stops being a haunting and starts being a bill — and bills can be paid. Late love is still love, but it has to say 'late' out loud. Type the first one now, here, at 1:47 in the morning. Grief keeps worse hours than you do."

TEE: "'I'm sorry I never said this. Your dad mattered to me. I read your message the day you sent it and I froze. I'm here now, late, but here.' Sent. She's typing back — and she's kind. Why is she kind?"

THE COLLECTOR: "You earned a door. Walk through it with the other ten, and take this: Mendie, a real Betty Plush soul, tag on. Mendie's your receipt — mending is a verb, admin. Pin her where the silence was pinned. We collect souls. Now we sell souls — and sometimes the soul we hand back is your own, paid up."

THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."

THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (—) L 94 (+3) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.4 ▲

TEE: P 48 (+2) L 58 (+14) T 28 (-14) │ PLT 45.2 ▲

*Analyst: The debt is collected where it was incurred — a sent message, in the chat, before witnesses.*

**FINAL SCORE**

THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.4 · P 72 · L 94 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"

TEE — PLT 45.2 · P 48 · L 58 · T 28 — ◆◆ "FINALLY PRESSED SEND"

**THE MOVE — "RULING BY BLUE TICK"**
*Tee's play: Tee ran silence as administration, reading every message within minutes and answering none of the hard ones, so the blue ticks did his ruling for him and no reply could ever be quoted against him. The maxim: "A ruler who only watches still rules — the watched learn to speak to somebody else." The Collector's answer: MAKE HIM PRESS SEND — She makes him pay the debt where it was incurred, sending the first late condolence live in the chat so presence, not drafting, becomes the record.*

**Takeaway:** *"A short true sentence sent beats a beautiful sentence drafted — presence is a streak, not an event."*

### TC8.2 — The Live Confession

*On Nova Reyes's live set at midnight — ring lights, donation alerts chirping, a tip-goal banner reading CONFESS SOMETHING REAL; THE COLLECTOR walks into frame mid-stream in neon streetwear, a cropped jacket with a glowing 222, her ornate scythe with a ring-light clipped to the shaft, phone in hand, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm photobombing every frame, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book under her arm, BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding reflected in every monitor.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · NOVA REYES (streamer who confesses strangers' secrets on live for donations)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

NOVA REYES — P 78 · L 28 · T 62 │ PLT 31.6

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

NOVA REYES: "Chat, we have a walk-in? Ma'am, closed set — is that a scythe with a ring light?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Clipped it on myself. When in the live, baby. I'm The Collector, Soul Collection Agency — Betty Plush, 222, PLT. Not a walk-in: a collection. Reading your page aloud, since aloud is your love language. Profit seventy-eight. Love twenty-eight. Tax sixty-two. PLT thirty-one point six."

NOVA REYES: "Profit seventy-eight is elite, though."

THE COLLECTOR: "Profit built from other people's pages is borrowed. Your tip jar promises a room — 'confess something real and I'll hold space.' You gave them a stadium, a donation alert like a cash register clearing its throat, and kept the gate."

THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲

NOVA REYES: P 78 (—) L 28 (—) T 65 (+3) │ PLT 30.4 ▼

*Analyst: The Reading reframes her format in one move: consent to be heard is not consent to be sold. Her Tax climbs as the audience hears the entry read back.*

**Beat 2 — SECRET FOR SALE**

NOVA REYES: "People say it heals them, hearing they're not alone!"

THE COLLECTOR: "Hearing you're not alone heals. Hearing your exact story read in lashes while a progress bar fills — that bills. Price one: Tuesday's night-shift nurse, her brother's overdose, her donation forty dollars, the clip ninety thousand views on the app. You call it healing content. The book calls it her Tax, converted at your exchange rate into your Profit."

NOVA REYES: "She sent it anonymously."

THE COLLECTOR: "Anonymously to you. You added the details back in like seasoning. You found people whose ledgers were too heavy to carry alone, and instead of helping them name their Tax, you rented it out by the minute."

THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 69.2 ▲

NOVA REYES: P 74 (-4) L 30 (+2) T 65 (—) │ PLT 29.6 ▼

*Analyst: One confession, priced end-to-end — forty dollars in, ninety thousand views out — becomes a conversion rate Nova must own.*

**Beat 3 — OTHER PEOPLE'S TAX**

NOVA REYES: "Half my regulars say this live is the only place they feel real."

THE COLLECTOR: "I believe them — that's the saddest line in your book. PLT taught straight, on your own camera: Profit is what you earn. Love is what you return. Tax is what it costs everybody when the first two don't balance. Your Love is thirty because in four years of nightly lives, you have never once confessed yours. Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."

NOVA REYES: "Don't — please don't read mine. Not on live."

THE COLLECTOR: "I don't read unconsented pages on camera. Remember that feeling, Nova. Bottle it. That's the whole sermon — it's the protection your confessors never got, and I'm handing it to you for free, on your own stream."

THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 92 (+3) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.8 ▲

NOVA REYES: P 72 (-2) L 38 (+8) T 58 (-7) │ PLT 34.8 ▲

*Analyst: The Collector proves the principle by observing it — declining to expose Nova's own secret gives her the protection her confessors never got, and the Love column moves first.*

**Beat 4 — THE MUTE BUTTON**

NOVA REYES: "If I stop the confessions, the channel dies. Chat, tell her you want them."

THE COLLECTOR: "Read your own chat, host. 'Keep going.' 'Realest stream in months.' 'Nova, confess yours.' They aren't asking for more secrets. They're asking for a first honest one — from you."

NOVA REYES: "Mine is about my sister. Before the format, early, I sold a story that was hers. She knew. That's why there's no sister in my life — just a sister-shaped story in my highlights. I owe her a call I can't make back with money."

THE COLLECTOR: "No alert, no tip, no progress bar — and it's the most valuable thing ever said on this channel, because it's yours and it cost you something. The mute button has never once worked on a ledger, Nova — not on a microphone's owner, not on a sister, not on you."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+2) L 92 (—) T 21 (—) │ PLT 71.6 ▲

NOVA REYES: P 70 (-2) L 48 (+10) T 50 (-8) │ PLT 41.2 ▲

*Analyst: Her own buried confession surfaces off-script and unpaid, worth more Love in one minute than four years of other people's — the format's premise quietly reverses.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**

THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement. Plain words, host. The camera can stay on. This part's yours."

NOVA REYES: "I took other people's Tax — their worst, heaviest pages — and monetised it as my Profit and called it healing. I never paid my own. I owe my sister the first confession, privately, with no audience, and I owe every person whose secret I sold a takedown and their money back."

THE COLLECTOR: "Named. Now collected: the confession inbox closes tonight, the clips come down — all of them — the donation ledger gets published, and your sister gets a call before she gets a clip. And look — chat is donating to the takedown fund, no secret required. People can give without bleeding, Nova. Take this: Listen, a real Betty Plush soul, tag on. Your receipt — listening that doesn't sell anything is your new format, and it's a better show."

NOVA REYES: "Fifty-four. I started at thirty-one with a full tip jar."

THE COLLECTOR: "Full jars are heavy, baby. We collect souls. Now we sell souls. Yours just got handed back, receipt in hand."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (—) L 95 (+3) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 73.2 ▲

NOVA REYES: P 68 (-2) L 64 (+16) T 32 (-18) │ PLT 54.0 ▲

*Analyst: The Settlement is structural — inbox closed, clips down, ledger published, sister called off-camera.*

**FINAL SCORE**

THE COLLECTOR — PLT 73.2 · P 73 · L 95 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"

NOVA REYES — PLT 54.0 · P 68 · L 64 · T 32 — ◆◆ "HER OWN STORY, AT LAST"

**THE MOVE — "THE BORROWED CONFESSION"**
*Nova Reyes's play: Nova ran the borrowed confession, performing strangers' secrets on live as her healing and pricing other people's pain gift by gift. The maxim: "Borrowed pain spends fast and leaves the borrower emptier than the lender." The Collector's answer: MAKE HER CONFESS HER OWN — She refuses to read Nova's own page on camera and forces the first real confession to be Nova's own, off the tip jar and aimed at her sister.*

**Takeaway:** *"A confession is only healing if it costs the teller something — other people's Tax is not your Profit."*

### TC8.3 — The Follower Count

*Inside Micah Vale's analytics dashboard come to life — follower graphs climbing the walls, notification bells raining softly, his count ticking on a stadium scoreboard; THE COLLECTOR strolls across the graph in neon streetwear, a cropped jacket with a glowing 222, her ornate scythe with a ring-light clipped to the shaft, phone in hand, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm photobombing every frame, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book open, BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding lit on the scoreboard beneath his count.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · MICAH VALE (influencer whose follower count is his whole Profit column)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

MICAH VALE — P 85 · L 22 · T 55 │ PLT 34.8

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

MICAH VALE: "Whoever you are, you're standing on my growth chart. That line took three years. Love the fit, though — the scythe is a strong silhouette."

THE COLLECTOR: "Micah Vale. Always measuring — even an intruder gets rated before she gets a hello. The Collector, Soul Collection Agency. Betty Plush, 222, PLT. Your count is public; your book is not, so allow me. Profit eighty-five. Love twenty-two. Tax fifty-five. PLT thirty-four point eight."

MICAH VALE: "Thirty-four? With eighty-five Profit? Your math is broken. I'm the biggest thing in my niche — two point one million, verify me."

THE COLLECTOR: "My math has buried bigger things than your niche, baby. Eighty-five Profit beside twenty-two Love isn't a brand, it's a tower on one leg — and Tax fifty-five is the wobble you feel at 3 a.m., checking the count like a pulse. The book don't blink."

THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 22 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲

MICAH VALE: P 85 (—) L 22 (—) T 58 (+3) │ PLT 33.6 ▼

*Analyst: The Reading pits his two numbers against each other — the public count he displays and the private PLT he has never computed. The gap between them is the debt.*

**Beat 2 — THE AUDIT**

MICAH VALE: "Audit fairly, then. That eighty-five is real work — three years, every day. I can tell you any creator's numbers cold. KetoKev, 890K, engagement down twelve percent since August. Mama Rosa Cooks, 3.4 million, comments forty percent bots."

THE COLLECTOR: "Stop. Hear yourself: you audited everyone else's numbers and never your own. One question, auditor — a number, not a vibe: when did somebody who can do nothing for your count last hear from you?"

MICAH VALE: "My mum leaves voice notes. I listen while I edit."

THE COLLECTOR: "At what speed, Micah?"

MICAH VALE: "...Double. Sometimes one-point-five."

THE COLLECTOR: "Kev gets your full attention at natural tempo. Your mother gets fast-forward."

THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+2) L 89 (—) T 22 (—) │ PLT 69.6 ▲

MICAH VALE: P 82 (-3) L 24 (+2) T 58 (—) │ PLT 33.2 ▼

*Analyst: His expertise becomes the evidence — total recall of rivals' metrics beside a blank on his own last deposit proves the audit was always pointed outward.*

**Beat 3 — NO DEPOSIT**

THE COLLECTOR: "Let's date the drought. Your first viral post, nineteen months ago — the book marks it in gold. Your Love column marks it too: last organic deposit, same week."

MICAH VALE: "People love me constantly. Look at the comments!"

THE COLLECTOR: "Those aren't deposits, they're transactions wearing hearts. A deposit is love that isn't buying anything and can't be screenshotted for a sponsor. Your brother's kid was born fourteen months ago. You've met her twice, both times with a ring light in your bag. She calls you Phone Man. Phone Man is a Tax receipt, baby."

MICAH VALE: "Okay. That's — bad."

THE COLLECTOR: "The column didn't empty in a day. It emptied in a streak — a birth, a birthday, a friend's bad week, every month a deposit slip you were too live to sign."

THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (—) L 91 (+2) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.8 ▲

MICAH VALE: P 80 (-2) L 32 (+8) T 52 (-6) │ PLT 38.0 ▲

*Analyst: She dates the Love drought to the viral week itself — the count went up and the column stopped, not despite his success but as its price.*

**Beat 4 — REAL LIFE COLUMN**

MICAH VALE: "So I quit? Delete the app, burn three years?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Check my jacket, influencer — 222, glowing. Nobody here is anti-Profit; eighty-five is a gift. The book is anti-tower-on-one-leg. The play is deposits where the camera isn't. Your friend Dev held your phone for that first viral video. He's carried his father's illness alone for five months, because he told you once, in a DM, and you replied with fire emojis and a voice note: 'Content will come from this, bro.'"

MICAH VALE: "I thought I was encouraging him."

THE COLLECTOR: "You were mining him — gently, with love emojis, but mining. Real-life column rule: care that can't be counted is the only kind that counts. If it would make good content, it first has to be good care — off the record, unposted, uncounted."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+1) L 91 (—) T 21 (—) │ PLT 71.2 ▲

MICAH VALE: P 78 (-2) L 44 (+12) T 46 (-6) │ PLT 44.4 ▲

*Analyst: She refuses the false exit of quitting. Profit is not the villain; the missing column is — and Dev gives him a concrete, recent, repairable harm.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**

THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement. The auditor audits himself — out loud, on his own scoreboard."

MICAH VALE: "I audited everyone else's numbers and never my own. My follower count became my whole Profit, and my real-life Love column went nineteen months without a deposit — my mum at double speed, my niece calling me Phone Man, my best friend grieving in my DMs while I looked for the angle. The count isn't my worth. It's just the loudest of my three numbers."

THE COLLECTOR: "Named, beautifully kept. Now the payment plan — none of it posts. Dev gets a visit: phone in your pocket, hospital corridor, unrecorded. Your mother gets a call at natural speed. The niece gets an uncle with full attention. And take this: Denim Patch, a real Betty Plush soul, tag on. Your receipt — patched, worn-in, real-life. Keep him off camera a month."

MICAH VALE: "PLT fifty-eight. First number of mine that's ever gone up while nobody was watching."

THE COLLECTOR: "That's how you know it's yours, baby. We collect souls. Now we sell souls — even ones with two point one million witnesses get handed back quietly."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (—) L 94 (+3) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.8 ▲

MICAH VALE: P 76 (-2) L 62 (+18) T 28 (-18) │ PLT 58.0 ▲

*Analyst: The Settlement inverts his economy: the whole payment plan is unposted, and his PLT makes its biggest jump precisely where no audience can verify it.*

**FINAL SCORE**

THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.8 · P 73 · L 94 · T 20 — ◆◆◆◆ "ALWAYS RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"

MICAH VALE — PLT 58.0 · P 76 · L 62 · T 28 — ◆◆◆ "MORE THAN THE COUNT"

**THE MOVE — "THE MIRROR TURNED OUTWARD"**
*Micah Vale's play: Micah ran the mirror turned outward, auditing every rival's numbers in public while his own Love column went nineteen months unread. The maxim: "A man who measures everybody but himself is not informed — he is hiding in arithmetic." The Collector's answer: TURN THE AUDIT INWARD — She runs his favourite audit on him, dating the Love drought to his viral week and sentencing him to deposits no camera can count.*

**Takeaway:** *"A follower count is only one of your three numbers — care that can't be counted is the only kind that counts."*

### TC8.4 — The Cancelled One

*A dark comedy club after closing, inside the app where his apology clips loop forever — one stool, one brick wall, a phone on a tripod still glowing; THE COLLECTOR takes the front-row seat in neon streetwear, a cropped jacket with a glowing 222, her ornate scythe with a ring-light clipped to the shaft laid across two chairs, phone in hand, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm photobombing every frame, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book on her lap, BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding chalked on the brick wall behind the mic.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · DRE FULLER (comedian cancelled two years ago, performing the apology tour nightly)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

DRE FULLER — P 38 · L 35 · T 68 │ PLT 16.0

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

DRE FULLER: "Front row, one lady, glowing jacket, farm equipment. Ma'am, the club's closed. You are officially my smallest and best-dressed audience."

THE COLLECTOR: "And your most honest one, Dre Fuller. The Collector, Soul Collection Agency — Betty Plush, 222, PLT. Two years you've done a set nightly for an audience of one phone. I'm the first person to attend in the flesh. Reading your page: Profit thirty-eight. Love thirty-five. Tax sixty-eight. PLT sixteen point zero."

DRE FULLER: "Sixteen. I've been cancelled by professionals with kinder numbers."

THE COLLECTOR: "Sixteen isn't a cancellation, baby. It's a stall. And Tax sixty-eight, the heaviest in this chapter — yours is the only debt here paid nightly in the wrong currency."

DRE FULLER: "Wrong currency? I apologised four hundred times. There's a tour. There's a hoodie."

THE COLLECTOR: "I know. The hoodie is in the book. Under exhibits."

**THE MOVE — "REMORSE ON REPEAT"**
*Dre Fuller's play: Dre ran remorse on repeat, performing his apology nightly so well that the performance itself became the reason no real payment was ever due. ## CHAPTER NINE — The Family Table

### TC9.1 — The Empty Chair

*Sunday dinner at Uncle Ray's house, the long table set for one too many: the Collector in a warm burgundy-and-gold Sunday-dinner fit, hair down, an apron with a PLT pocket over the dress, her ornate scythe leaned by the kitchen door — even she doesn't carry it to the table — Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book open beside the serving bowl, BETTY PLUSH on the 222 napkin rings and PLT stitched gold on the table runner.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · UNCLE RAY (the family-table elder, keeper of his late brother's empty chair)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
UNCLE RAY — P 58 · L 62 · T 48 │ PLT 42.8

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
UNCLE RAY: "You set that book by my good bowl on purpose."
THE COLLECTOR: "I set it where the truth already sits, Uncle. Listen to your page. PROFIT FIFTY-EIGHT. LOVE SIXTY-TWO. TAX FORTY-EIGHT. PLT forty-two point eight."
UNCLE RAY: "Forty-eight Tax? I don't owe nobody forty-eight of nothing."
THE COLLECTOR: "You owe it in chairs. That empty seat at the head has been collecting from this whole table for nine years."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 88 (—) T 24 (+2) │ PLT 67.6 ▼
UNCLE RAY: P 58 (—) L 60 (-2) T 48 (—) │ PLT 42.0 ▼
*Analyst: The Reading lands soft and exact — the Collector names the Tax as seating, not sin, and Ray's Love dips the moment he hears his grief priced as something other people pay.*

**Beat 2 — THE NINE-YEAR SEAT**
UNCLE RAY: "That's Elias's chair. My brother's chair. A man don't give his brother's chair to just anybody with feet."
THE COLLECTOR: "Nobody's asking you to give it away. I'm asking who pays for keeping it empty."
UNCLE RAY: "PAYS? WE ALL LOVED HIM."
THE COLLECTOR: "You did. And every Sunday since, the children eat standing up in the kitchen so a ghost can have elbow room."
UNCLE RAY: "They never said nothing."
THE COLLECTOR: "Family rarely invoices out loud, Ray. That's why the Agency sends me."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 90 (+2) T 24 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
UNCLE RAY: P 57 (-1) L 62 (+2) T 50 (+2) │ PLT 41.6 ▼
*Analyst: Ray defends the chair as loyalty and his Tax rises — grief defended becomes grief charged twice, once to him and once to everyone standing in the kitchen.*

**Beat 3 — THE BROTHER'S NAME**
THE COLLECTOR: "Tell me one true thing about Elias that isn't his death."
UNCLE RAY: "He… he used to steal the crispy skin off the chicken and blame the dog. We ain't never had a dog."
THE COLLECTOR: "THERE HE IS. THAT'S THE BROTHER."
UNCLE RAY: "Lord, I ain't said that out loud in — Elias would've laughed himself under this table."
THE COLLECTOR: "Notice what just happened. You remembered the man, not the funeral, and the room got lighter, not heavier."
UNCLE RAY: "Feels wrong, laughing at his table."
THE COLLECTOR: "It feels new. Wrong and new get misfiled together all the time."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 92 (+2) T 25 (+1) │ PLT 69.2 ▲
UNCLE RAY: P 59 (+2) L 66 (+4) T 48 (-2) │ PLT 44.8 ▲
*Analyst: The Collector separates the man from the mourning — the first real memory in nine years deposits Love and Profit at once, because a told story is value a shrine can never pay.*

**Beat 4 — THE WHOLE TABLE PAYS**
UNCLE RAY: "If I let somebody sit there, it's like saying nine years didn't matter."
THE COLLECTOR: "No. Keeping it empty says the nine years are ALL that matters — and your great-niece eats off the counter."
UNCLE RAY: "She likes the counter!"
THE COLLECTOR: "She's seven, Ray. She likes anywhere she's fed. The Tax isn't your love for Elias. THE TAX IS THE SILENCE AROUND THE CHAIR."
THE COLLECTOR: "Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."
UNCLE RAY: "…Everybody walks past it like it's a hole in the floor."
THE COLLECTOR: "Because you taught them to. Grief nobody may touch becomes a bill nobody may question."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (-1) L 90 (-2) T 27 (+2) │ PLT 67.2 ▼
UNCLE RAY: P 60 (+1) L 70 (+4) T 44 (-4) │ PLT 48.4 ▲
*Analyst: Family costs the Collector too — her score dips telling an elder his monument is a levy — but naming the Tax as silence, not love, lets Ray keep the brother and drop the bill.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement, plain. The debt is grief kept as a seating charge — nine years, collected from every plate, every child standing, every laugh swallowed at this table. It's due, and it's done."
UNCLE RAY: "And Elias?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Elias gets a photograph on the sideboard and his chicken story told every Sunday. The chair gets a living person. MOVE THE CHAIR, KEEP THE MAN."
UNCLE RAY: "…Pull it up, then. Somebody sit down before the greens get cold. Elias, you heard the lady — scoot."
THE COLLECTOR: "Paid. And your receipt — Nightwing Dawn, tagged and true. For the one who kept the night watch. Your watch is over, Uncle."
UNCLE RAY: "Huh. Little fella's got wings. Elias would've stole him too."
THE COLLECTOR: P 72 (+2) L 93 (+3) T 25 (-2) │ PLT 70.0 ▲
UNCLE RAY: P 62 (+2) L 78 (+8) T 28 (-16) │ PLT 58.8 ▲
*Analyst: The Settlement is the chapter in one move — the biggest Tax drop at any family table comes not from forgetting but from relocating: memory to the sideboard, life back to the chair.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 70.0 · P 72 · L 93 · T 25 — ◆◆◆◆ "RIGHT ABOUT THE NUMBER"
UNCLE RAY — PLT 58.8 · P 62 · L 78 · T 28 — ◆◆◆ "CHAIR FILLED, BROTHER KEPT"

**THE MOVE — "THE CHAIR THAT GOVERNS"**
*Uncle Ray's play: Keeping his late brother's chair empty for nine years turned one man's grief into the seating law the whole family's Sundays had to obey. The maxim: "When sorrow is given the head seat, joy learns to eat standing up." The Collector's answer: MOVE THE CHAIR, KEEP THE MAN — the Reading prices the silence around the seat, and the Settlement moves Elias into story and photograph so a living person can sit down.*

**Takeaway:** *"Grief is love with nowhere to sit — give it a story, not a chair."*

### TC9.2 — The Recipe Book

*The Collector's second stop, Aunt Pearline's kitchen at golden hour, the family recipe book chained by habit to the counter: the Collector in a warm burgundy-and-gold Sunday-dinner fit, hair down, an apron with a PLT pocket over the dress, her ornate scythe leaned by the kitchen door — even she doesn't carry it to the table — Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book beside the recipe book, BETTY PLUSH on the 222 oven mitt and PLT embroidered on the apron Pearline lent no one, ever.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · AUNT PEARLINE (keeper of the family recipe book, who measures love in portions)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
AUNT PEARLINE — P 64 · L 70 · T 38 │ PLT 52.4

AUNT PEARLINE: "Wipe your feet. That pink book better not be bringing me bad news in my own kitchen."
THE COLLECTOR: "It brings your own numbers, Auntie. Profit sixty-four. Love seventy. Tax thirty-eight. PLT fifty-two point four."
AUNT PEARLINE: "Love seventy? I feed this entire family. Love ought to be a hundred and then some."
THE COLLECTOR: "Love served is a hundred. LOVE SERVED SELECTIVELY GETS AUDITED."
AUNT PEARLINE: "Selective? Child, pass me my spoon before I season you."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 89 (+1) T 23 (+1) │ PLT 68.4 ─
AUNT PEARLINE: P 64 (—) L 68 (-2) T 38 (—) │ PLT 51.6 ▼
*Analyst: The Reading draws the book's sharpest distinction — Pearline's Love is real but rationed, and a rationed deposit is a withdrawal wearing an apron.*

**Beat 2 — THE MEASURED LOVE**
THE COLLECTOR: "Your book says: serves twelve. Your pot, last three Thanksgivings, served ten and a half."
AUNT PEARLINE: "A half?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Denise's branch gets a plate. A SMALL PLATE. LITERALLY A SAUCER, PEARLINE."
AUNT PEARLINE: "That woman stood at her own brother's funeral and talked about his will over my potato salad!"
THE COLLECTOR: "That was 2016."
AUNT PEARLINE: "And potato salad has a memory!"
THE COLLECTOR: "So the sentence is nine years of saucers, served cold, to her and her children — who argued about no will, because they were nine."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 89 (—) T 23 (—) │ PLT 68.8 ▲
AUNT PEARLINE: P 63 (-1) L 68 (—) T 40 (+2) │ PLT 50.4 ▼
*Analyst: Portion control as punishment — every cut serving is a small, deniable withdrawal, and PLT catches what the family politely pretended was just a smaller plate.*

**Beat 3 — THE MISSING PLATE**
AUNT PEARLINE: "You think I don't know her kids notice? I know. I watch them notice. That's the — oh."
THE COLLECTOR: "Say the rest, Auntie. The book leaves room."
AUNT PEARLINE: "THAT'S THE POINT OF THE SAUCER. LORD, THAT'S UGLY SAID OUT LOUD."
THE COLLECTOR: "Out loud is where ugly loses its discount. In silence it charges full price."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book don't blink, Auntie."
AUNT PEARLINE: "I kept cooking for everybody else. I thought that balanced it."
THE COLLECTOR: "Feeding ten people generously does not redeposit what you withdrew from two. Love doesn't average, Pearline. It itemizes."
THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (—) L 91 (+2) T 24 (+1) │ PLT 69.2 ▲
AUNT PEARLINE: P 65 (+2) L 72 (+4) T 38 (-2) │ PLT 53.6 ▲
*Analyst: The confession is the first deposit — Pearline admitting the saucer was the message turns a hidden Tax into a named one, and named debts are the only kind that can be settled.*

**Beat 4 — THE FUNERAL ARGUMENT**
AUNT PEARLINE: "She humiliated this family in a house of mourning."
THE COLLECTOR: "She did. And you answered a one-day offence with a nine-year sentence, AND YOU MADE THE CHILDREN SERVE IT WITH HER."
AUNT PEARLINE: "Her children eat!"
THE COLLECTOR: "Off saucers. At the big table. In front of cousins with plates. You didn't punish Denise, Auntie — you taxed her bloodline and called it recipes."
AUNT PEARLINE: "…My mama wrote 'feed everybody' on the first page of that book. In pencil. Like she knew."
THE COLLECTOR: "She knew. That's why the book came to you — and why this page is in mine."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (-1) L 89 (-2) T 26 (+2) │ PLT 67.2 ▼
AUNT PEARLINE: P 66 (+1) L 76 (+4) T 35 (-3) │ PLT 56.8 ▲
*Analyst: The Collector refuses the easy acquittal and the easy condemnation — Pearline's grievance was real, but a real grievance collected from children compounds into the family's most expensive kind of Tax.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement, plain. The debt is love withdrawn one plate at a time since 2016 — nine years of saucers, owed to Denise's branch, with interest paid by children who never signed for it. It's due, and it's done."
AUNT PEARLINE: "What do I do, just — cook?"
THE COLLECTOR: "You write her name back in the book. Full page, full portions, in ink. And this Sunday, YOU SET THE BIG PLATES FIRST — HERS."
AUNT PEARLINE: "She'll think it's a trap."
THE COLLECTOR: "Then eat off a saucer yourself, beside her, until she believes you. Mend it at the table where you broke it."
AUNT PEARLINE: "…Hand me that pen. The ink one. And child — this one's receipt better be pretty."

AUNT PEARLINE: P 68 (+2) L 84 (+8) T 22 (-13) │ PLT 66.0 ▲
*Analyst: Restitution in kind — the debt was paid out in portions, so it is repaid in portions: a name restored in ink and the big plates set first, where the whole table can audit the deposit.*

AUNT PEARLINE — PLT 66.0 · P 68 · L 84 · T 22 — ◆◆◆ "FULL PLATES, FULL FAMILY"

**THE MOVE — "THE LADLE DECIDES"**
*Aunt Pearline's play: Keeping the recipe book and the servings in her own hands let her measure love in portions and quietly cut Denise's branch down to saucers since 2016. The maxim: "A helping made smaller on purpose teaches the whole table who is being forgiven and who is not." The Collector's answer: FULL PLATES FIRST — the Reading itemizes the cut portions, and the Settlement restores Denise's name in ink and sets her big plates first where everyone can see.*

**Takeaway:** *"Love doesn't average — it itemizes, and every small plate is a line in the book."*

MARCUS: "…The living one. The dead one's finished hurting her."

THE COLLECTOR: "Settled. Paid in presence. Your receipt — Listen, tagged and true. The soul that hears the house. You don't have to talk first, eldest. Just be in the room where you're loved."

MARCUS: P 80 (-2) L 72 (+11) T 18 (-14) │ PLT 67.6 ▲

MARCUS — PLT 67.6 · P 80 · L 72 · T 18 — ◆◆◆ "CAME HOME, SAT DOWN"

*Marcus's play: Sending perfect monthly deposits home bought him a standing excuse to miss the table itself for six years. The maxim: "A bill paid early cannot excuse a chair left empty on purpose." ### TC9.4 — Mama's Ledger

*After dinner, the kitchen quiet, dishes done by everybody but the two women at the head of the long table: the Collector in a warm burgundy-and-gold Sunday-dinner fit, hair down, an apron with a PLT pocket over the dress, her ornate scythe leaned by the kitchen door — even she doesn't carry it to the table — Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, the pink collection book closed between them like a held breath, BETTY PLUSH on the 222 lamp shade and PLT in gold thread down the runner, forty years of accounts in the room.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · MAMA LOU (the family's matriarch, who has carried the family's whole book alone)

**OPENING HOLDING**
THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4
MAMA LOU — P 68 · L 92 · T 44 │ PLT 60.4

**Beat 1 — THE READING**
THE COLLECTOR: "Last page of the route, Mama. Your page. Profit sixty-eight. Love ninety-two — highest in this book, and it isn't close. Tax forty-four. PLT sixty point four."
MAMA LOU: "Ninety-two Love and a Tax like a mortgage. Read it again, baby. SLOWER. I WANT TO HEAR WHERE A MOTHER BLEEDS."
THE COLLECTOR: "You bleed in the carrying, Mama. Forty-four is—"
MAMA LOU: "Forty-four is forty years of everybody's book in my apron pocket. I know what it is. I been knowing. Sit up straight — you're slouching over my ledger."
THE COLLECTOR: "I'm the Collector, Mama."
MAMA LOU: "And I'm the reason there's anything in this family worth collecting. Proceed."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (—) L 88 (—) T 24 (+2) │ PLT 67.6 ▼
MAMA LOU: P 68 (—) L 92 (—) T 44 (—) │ PLT 60.4 ─
*Analyst: The Reading reverses on contact — Mama Lou's ledger was never a secret to the woman who wrote it in her own body.*

**Beat 2 — MAMA READS BACK**
MAMA LOU: "Now yours. You read everybody at my table tonight. Ray's chair, Pearline's saucers, Marcus's airplane. Very fine work. Now read the page you skipped."
THE COLLECTOR: "My page isn't on this route."
MAMA LOU: "YOUR PAGE, BABY. I TAUGHT YOU THE NUMBERS. DON'T LIE TO THE TEACHER."
THE COLLECTOR: "…Collector. Profit high. Love given — very high. Love received, deposited back, allowed to land — the column's thin, Mama."
THE COLLECTOR: "The book don't blink. Not even for me."
MAMA LOU: "Thin as church lemonade. You collect for the whole world and come home empty-handed on purpose. I raised a banker who won't take a deposit."
THE COLLECTOR: "If I stop to be collected for, the route—"
MAMA LOU: "The route will keep. Routes do. Daughters don't always."
THE COLLECTOR: P 69 (-1) L 86 (-2) T 27 (+3) │ PLT 65.2 ▼
MAMA LOU: P 69 (+1) L 93 (+1) T 44 (—) │ PLT 61.2 ▲
*Analyst: The summit turns — Mama Lou reads the Collector with her own method, and the un-collectable collector is exposed as a family pattern, learned at this table.*

**Beat 3 — THE BOOK SHE CARRIED ALONE**
THE COLLECTOR: "Then hear yours whole, Mama. You carried this family's book alone for forty years. Every grief, every saucer, every empty chair — audited by one woman, at night, for free."
MAMA LOU: "Somebody had to."
THE COLLECTOR: "SOMEBODY DID. IT WAS YOU. IT WAS ONLY EVER YOU. That's the debt, Mama — not that you carried it. That you never let it be carried with you."
MAMA LOU: "A mother hands her children a book like this, they drop it."
THE COLLECTOR: "We dropped nothing tonight. Ray sat a stranger in a sacred chair. Pearline picked up a pen. Marcus sat down. The family can hold pages now, Mama. You trained us at this table and never graduated us."
MAMA LOU: "…Graduated. Hmph. I did like being the only one who could read it."
THE COLLECTOR: "I know. So do I. Wonder where I got it."
THE COLLECTOR: P 69 (—) L 88 (+2) T 27 (—) │ PLT 66.0 ▲
MAMA LOU: P 67 (-2) L 95 (+2) T 42 (-2) │ PLT 62.0 ▲
*Analyst: Sole custody of the ledger felt like love and functioned as control — and 'I liked being the only one who could read it' is the most honest line at the table.*

**Beat 4 — THE COLLECTOR'S OWN PAGE**
MAMA LOU: "Then we settle honest, both directions. Mine first: I carried it alone because alone was proof. If I set it down, who'd know what it cost?"
THE COLLECTOR: "We know, Mama. We always knew. We just weren't allowed to pay any of it back."
MAMA LOU: "And yours, baby. Say yours plain, like you make them say it."
THE COLLECTOR: "I COLLECT FOR EVERYONE AND LET NO ONE COLLECT FOR ME. If love comes for me, I audit it. I check its tags. I find a reason it's not due."
MAMA LOU: "Because if you let it land—"
THE COLLECTOR: "I'd have to sit in a chair somebody else set for me. Like Marcus. Like Ray. I am this family's daughter, Mama. The pattern has my name on the spine."
MAMA LOU: "There she is. There's my girl under the Agency."
THE COLLECTOR: P 67 (-2) L 88 (—) T 30 (+3) │ PLT 64.0 ▼
MAMA LOU: P 67 (—) L 97 (+2) T 40 (-2) │ PLT 63.6 ▲
*Analyst: Naming her own debt in her mother's kitchen drops the Collector to 64.0, her lowest score in the book — proof that at this table she is not staff, she is kin.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**
THE COLLECTOR: "Settlement, both ways, plain. Mama — your debt is the book carried alone: forty years of sole custody, settled tonight by distribution. Ray holds the grief page. Pearline the feeding page. Marcus the home page. You hold the head of the table — not the whole table's book."
MAMA LOU: "And the interest?"
THE COLLECTOR: "Paid in full by three settlements you watched from your own chair. Now mine — named, not yet paid: I collect for everyone and let no one collect for me. It goes in the book tonight, in ink, due next. The Agency's last page is my own, and I will sit for it."
MAMA LOU: "YOU'LL SIT FOR IT BECAUSE I TAUGHT YOU: NOBODY'S SOUL IS TAKEN — AND NOBODY'S DEBT IS PAID — BY RUNNING."
THE COLLECTOR: "Receipt, Mama. Pixel Heart — tagged and true, the heart in full colour, for the woman who carried every soul in this family before there was an Agency to name it."
MAMA LOU: "And you take this — my apron. PLT pocket and all. When you sit for your own page, baby, wear the pocket on the outside. Let them reach you."
THE COLLECTOR: "Settled, Mama."
MAMA LOU: "Settled, Collector. Now eat something. You're thin in the Love column and I intend to fix the part of it that's mine."
THE COLLECTOR: P 70 (+3) L 93 (+5) T 28 (-2) │ PLT 68.0 ▲
MAMA LOU: P 70 (+3) L 100 (+3) T 24 (-16) │ PLT 72.4 ▲
*Analyst: The summit settles both ways — Mama's Tax collapses as the book is distributed page by page, and the Collector leaves with her own debt entered and due in Chapter Ten: the collector, at last, on the route.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 68.0 · P 70 · L 93 · T 28 — ◆◆◆ "COLLECTED BY HER OWN TABLE"
MAMA LOU — PLT 72.4 · P 70 · L 100 · T 24 — ◆◆◆◆ "THE BOOK, SHARED AT LAST"

**THE MOVE — "THE BOOK IN ONE PAIR OF HANDS"**
*Mama Lou's play: Carrying the family's whole book alone for forty years kept anyone else from ever opening it — and in this same sitting she names the Collector's own answering move, collecting for everyone while letting no one collect for her. The maxim: "Love that insists on carrying everything will one day make the carrying itself the proof." The Collector's answer: THE BOOK, SHARED BOTH WAYS — the Settlement distributes Mama's pages to Ray, Pearline, and Marcus, and enters the Collector's own debt in ink for her to sit for next, a mutual settlement in one sitting.*

**Takeaway:** *"The strongest hands at the table are the ones that finally let the book be carried by everybody."*

## CHAPTER TEN — The Collection Room

### TC10.1 — The Receipt Wall

*The Collection Room at the Soul Collection Agency after hours — pink-and-teal throne-room light, shelves of collected souls displayed as tagged products under the "COLLECTED SOULS — SECTION 222" plaque: Spike, Drippy, Mendie, Denim Patch, Ember Stripe, Pixel Heart, Bell Paws, Nightwing Dawn, Listen. THE COLLECTOR is home in full regalia — pink-and-gold polka-dot motif, crown-energy headpiece, ceremonial scythe resting against the throne, Drippy the pink drip bunny in her arm, the grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book open — BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding on the wall, the shelves, and her sleeves.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · WREN (young new Agency hire, keeper of the receipt wall)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

WREN — P 45 · L 60 · T 38 │ PLT 40.8

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

WREN: "Before you start — the wall's fine. I dusted. Nobody needs reading near the wall."

THE COLLECTOR: "Wren. Your page, out loud, like we do everybody: Profit forty-five, Love sixty, Tax thirty-eight. PLT forty point eight. That's a girl paying interest on a door she won't walk through. Name the Tax, or the Tax names you."

THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 90 (+2) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.0 ▲

WREN: P 44 (-1) L 58 (-2) T 41 (+3) │ PLT 38.4 ▼

*Analyst: The reading isn't an ambush — it's the job description. Wren's Tax spikes because she heard her own numbers in the room she thought was only for other people.*

**Beat 2 — THE SMILING FILES**

WREN: "Fine. Then explain this first, Collector. Why do the collected keep smiling in their files? Pearl Adeyemi, Ember Stripe file, third shelf — she's grinning like she won something. People who lose their souls aren't supposed to grin."

THE COLLECTOR: "Because nobody in this room loses, baby. A file smiles when the debt stops eating the person. Pearl didn't lose a soul on that shelf. She lost a bill she'd been carrying in her chest. Look closer — the tag says SETTLED, not TAKEN. Words matter in this agency. We are very picky about our verbs."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+2) L 91 (+1) T 21 (—) │ PLT 71.2 ▲

WREN: P 47 (+3) L 62 (+4) T 39 (-2) │ PLT 42.0 ▲

*Analyst: Wren came to audit the wall and found the wall's defense in one word: settled. Her Love rises first — she believes the files before she believes her own page.*

**Beat 3 — THE UNOPENED PAGE**

WREN: "Okay. But there's a page in my file with a paperclip on it and I did not put a paperclip on it."

THE COLLECTOR: "I did. That's the page where you tell the room why you really took this job. You didn't apply to the Soul Collection Agency because you love shelving, Wren. You have excellent shelving posture and terrible shelving motives."

WREN: "I took the job because— because if I'm the one holding the book, nobody opens mine. There. Is that — is that in the page?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Word for word, and you just read it better than I could. See how that works? The book doesn't drag it out of you. It waits till you're tired of carrying it in the dark."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (—) L 93 (+2) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.4 ▲

WREN: P 45 (-2) L 62 (—) T 43 (+4) │ PLT 39.6 ▼

*Analyst: Confession costs before it pays. Wren names the avoidance out loud and her Tax bills her for it immediately — honesty is invoiced before it's rewarded.*

**Beat 4 — THE JOB INTERVIEW THAT WASN'T**

WREN: "So the whole job was a trap? Dust the souls, learn the shelves, get read by the boss on a Tuesday?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Not a trap. A route. Everybody who works here walks it. Spike got read. I got read — ask Mama Lou, she'll tell it at volume. The agency doesn't hire people whose books are closed, Wren. We hire people whose books are due, and we let them earn while they open."

WREN: "And if I'd never opened it?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Then you'd have dusted other people's freedom for forty years and called it a career. We don't do haunted staff here. We do settled staff."

THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+1) L 94 (+1) T 19 (-1) │ PLT 73.6 ▲

WREN: P 49 (+4) L 65 (+3) T 40 (-3) │ PLT 43.6 ▲

*Analyst: The Collector reframes the hire as the agency's oldest policy: the staff get collected first. Wren's Profit jumps — she finally values the job she actually took.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**

WREN: "Then name it plain. What do I owe?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Plain, then. The debt is one reading, given honestly, by the person who took a job to avoid it. Paid tonight, in this room, in full. Wren — you owe nobody an apology for hiding. You owe yourself an open book. Consider it collected."

WREN: "That's it? No scythe? No lightning?"

THE COLLECTOR: "The scythe is ceremonial, sweetheart. The score does the collecting. And this —" *[she takes a small tagged Pixel Heart soul from the shelf and presses it into Wren's hands]* "— is your receipt. Pixel Heart. Tag stays on. When it feels heavy, read your page again. When it feels light — that's the job done right."

THE COLLECTOR: "Settled."

THE COLLECTOR: P 77 (+3) L 98 (+4) T 17 (-2) │ PLT 77.2 ▲

WREN: P 55 (+6) L 73 (+8) T 34 (-6) │ PLT 51.6 ▲

*Analyst: The biggest move lands where it always lands — the plain naming. Wren's settlement is the chapter's thesis in miniature: nothing was taken from her, and she is measurably lighter than when she walked in.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 77.2 · P 77 · L 98 · T 17 — ◆◆◆◆ "HOME AND HOLDING"
WREN — PLT 51.6 · P 55 · L 73 · T 34 — ◆◆ "READ AT LAST"
**THE MOVE — "THE DUSTER'S ALIBI"**
*Wren's play: She took the one job where she tends everyone else's pages so her own never comes due, turning service into a hiding place with a perfect attendance record. The maxim: "Hold the light for every other page long enough and no one will notice your own page was never lit." The Collector's answer: THE READING, OUT LOUD — She reads Wren's page aloud in the room Wren thought was only for other people and settles it with one honest reading paid in full.*
**Takeaway:** *"The files smile because nothing was taken — the weight was."*

### TC10.2 — The Misdelivered Soul

*The Collection Room, morning — pink-and-teal light off the throne, the "COLLECTED SOULS — SECTION 222" plaque polished, shelves of tagged souls (Spike, Drippy, Mendie, Denim Patch, Ember Stripe, Pixel Heart, Bell Paws, Nightwing Dawn, Listen) standing like a choir. THE COLLECTOR in full regalia — pink-and-gold polka-dot motif, crown-energy headpiece, ceremonial scythe across the desk like a ruler, Drippy in her arm, grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book open to a page with a crooked stamp — BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding on the desk seal and the wall.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · MR. FELIX AMARO (a man whose file arrived at the Agency by error — or so the stamp says)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

MR. FELIX AMARO — P 62 · L 55 · T 48 │ PLT 41.6

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

MR. FELIX AMARO: "I want it noted, for whatever record you're keeping, that I came here to return this file. It isn't mine. Well — it's got my name on it, but it isn't true."

THE COLLECTOR: "Noted. Now the reading, because we read before we argue — that's house rules, even for bad paper. Felix Amaro: Profit sixty-two, Love fifty-five, Tax forty-eight. PLT forty-one point six. Mr. Amaro, that's a man paying rent on a house he already bought."

THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 88 (—) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 69.2 ▲

MR. FELIX AMARO: P 61 (-1) L 54 (-1) T 51 (+3) │ PLT 39.6 ▼

*Analyst: The Collector reads the page as written — including its lie. Felix's Tax climbs because being misread, on the record, in a beautiful room, still costs like the truth does.*

**Beat 2 — PAID TWICE**

MR. FELIX AMARO: "That debt — the one with the red underline? I paid it. Nineteen years ago. In full, with witnesses, with a priest, with my mother holding the receipt in a Bible, which in my family is a safety-deposit box."

THE COLLECTOR: "And yet the page says open."

MR. FELIX AMARO: "The page is wrong. I paid it twice, Collector. Once when it was due, and once when a man with a stamp told me the first time didn't count because the office had moved. I paid a debt twice rather than let my name sit in a book like this one. And now it sits in yours."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+2) L 90 (+2) T 21 (—) │ PLT 70.8 ▲

MR. FELIX AMARO: P 63 (+2) L 55 (+1) T 49 (-2) │ PLT 41.6 ▲

*Analyst: Felix's numbers start crawling home the moment the facts hit the table: the debt is documented, doubled, and done. The book is the only party in the room still arguing.*

**Beat 3 — TWO CITIES, ONE RECEIPT**

THE COLLECTOR: "Two cities, you said. Tell it slow. The book likes slow. The book don't blink."

MR. FELIX AMARO: "Paid in the first city, where I owed it. Paid again in the second city, where I didn't — because a clerk told me gratitude travels but paperwork doesn't. So I carried both receipts for nineteen years, in case a third city asked. Do you know what nineteen years of in-case weighs, Collector?"

THE COLLECTOR: "I collect weight for a living, Mr. Amaro. Nineteen years of in-case is the heaviest thing in this building, and this building has a throne in it. Show me the second receipt."

MR. FELIX AMARO: *[producing a folded, soft-edged paper]* "Careful. It's older than your apprentice."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (—) L 93 (+3) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.4 ▲

MR. FELIX AMARO: P 66 (+3) L 57 (+2) T 46 (-3) │ PLT 44.8 ▲

*Analyst: The receipt matches. The Collector's Love rises — not for Felix's innocence, but for nineteen years of a man guarding his own good name with folded paper.*

**Beat 4 — THE STAMP THAT LIED**

MR. FELIX AMARO: "So. What does the Agency do when the Agency is wrong?"

THE COLLECTOR: *[a long look at the page]* "First, it says so out loud, in the room, on the record. The stamp lied, Mr. Amaro. Not the man — the stamp. This file was misdelivered, miscopied, and left open by an office that owed you a correction and sent you a summons instead. My book can be wrong about the record. Understand me — it is never wrong about the person. It read your weight right. It just billed the wrong account."

MR. FELIX AMARO: "Nineteen years, and that's the first time anybody from an office said the word 'wrong' before the word 'payment.'"

THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+1) L 94 (+1) T 22 (+2) │ PLT 72.4 ─

MR. FELIX AMARO: P 70 (+4) L 60 (+3) T 42 (-4) │ PLT 49.2 ▲

*Analyst: The admission costs the Collector Tax in real time — institutional honesty is never free — and her PLT holds flat while Felix's jumps. That's what an apology looks like on a scorebug: the apologizer pays.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**

THE COLLECTOR: "The settlement, plain. Felix Amaro owes this Agency nothing — he never did, on this page. The Agency owes Felix Amaro: one debt, paid twice, corrected once, tonight. I am collecting the error itself — the lie, the stamp, the nineteen years of in-case — and it comes off your book and goes on ours. And this —" *[she writes, large, in the pink collection book]* "— is the Agency's apology, booked as a deposit in your name. It earns. You can draw on it the next time any office in any city tells you your word isn't paper enough."

MR. FELIX AMARO: "You put apologies in the book as money?"

THE COLLECTOR: "We put everything true in the book, Mr. Amaro. That's the whole religion. And your receipt —" *[she hands him a small tagged Bell Paws soul from the shelf]* "— Bell Paws. For a man who carried receipts for nineteen years: let this one carry you. Tag stays on. File's closed. Smile for the shelf — everybody does, eventually."

THE COLLECTOR: P 78 (+4) L 99 (+5) T 19 (-3) │ PLT 77.2 ▲

MR. FELIX AMARO: P 77 (+7) L 69 (+9) T 35 (-7) │ PLT 58.4 ▲

*Analyst: The settlement reverses the flow of the entire series: the collector collects from her own institution. Felix leaves richer than the book ever billed him — because the book, corrected, finally agrees with the man.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 77.2 · P 78 · L 99 · T 19 — ◆◆◆◆ "THE BOOK CORRECTS ITSELF"
MR. FELIX AMARO — PLT 58.4 · P 77 · L 69 · T 35 — ◆◆◆ "PAID, TWICE, AND FREE"
**THE MOVE — "THE TWICE-PAID SHIELD"**
*Mr. Felix Amaro's play: He paid a settled debt twice over rather than ever be seen owing, wearing righteousness as armour while the Agency's false record let him keep paying instead of living. The maxim: "Pay what you no longer owe and pride will call it honour while it quietly rents you your own good name." The Collector's answer: COLLECT THE ERROR — She collects the Agency's own error itself, taking the lying stamp and nineteen years of in-case off his book and onto hers.*
**Takeaway:** *"The book can be wrong about the record — it is never wrong about the person."*

#### Episode Script — TC10.2

**Who's in the Room:** THE COLLECTOR in full regalia behind the Collection Room desk, ceremonial scythe laid flat, Drippy in her arm, Spike bookbag on her back; MR. FELIX AMARO, carrying nineteen years of receipts for a debt he already paid twice.

**Beats:** 1. THE READING — The Collector reads Felix's holding aloud: P 62, L 55, T 48, PLT 41.6 — a man paying rent on a house he already bought. 2. PAID TWICE — Felix testifies: the red-underlined debt was paid in full, then paid again when an office moved. 3. TWO CITIES, ONE RECEIPT — The soft-edged second receipt is produced and matches; nineteen years of "in-case" is weighed. 4. THE STAMP THAT LIED — The Collector admits it on the record: the stamp lied, not the man; her own Tax pays for the admission. 5. THE SETTLEMENT — The Agency owes him: the error itself is collected, the apology is booked as a deposit, and Felix receives a tagged Bell Paws soul as his receipt.

I PAID A DEBT TWICE RATHER THAN LET MY NAME SIT

NINETEEN YEARS OF IN-CASE

THE STAMP LIED — NOT THE MAN

THE AGENCY'S APOLOGY, BOOKED AS A DEPOSIT

**The Move:** "THE TWICE-PAID SHIELD" — *"Pay what you no longer owe and pride will call it honour while it quietly rents you your own good name."* The Collector answers with COLLECT THE ERROR.
