### TC10.3 — The Collector's Own Book

*The Collection Room, lights low — pink-and-teal throne-room glow, the "COLLECTED SOULS — SECTION 222" plaque catching it, shelves of tagged souls (Spike, Drippy, Mendie, Denim Patch, Ember Stripe, Pixel Heart, Bell Paws, Nightwing Dawn, Listen) quiet as a congregation. THE COLLECTOR in full regalia — pink-and-gold polka-dot motif, crown-energy headpiece, ceremonial scythe laid down, not held, for once — Drippy in her arm, grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, and the pink collection book open at its final section, the pages she has never opened on the record. BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding everywhere except, for once, in her voice. WREN holds the book.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · WREN (young Agency apprentice, keeper of the receipt wall — tonight, the reader)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

WREN — P 55 · L 73 · T 34 │ PLT 51.6

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

WREN: "House rules. We read before we argue. So — sit. No, sit in the good chair. You always stand, that's half the page already. Ready? The Collector: Profit seventy, Love eighty-eight, Tax twenty-two. PLT sixty-eight point four."

THE COLLECTOR: "And? Every reading has an 'and,' Wren. I taught you the 'and.'"

WREN: "And — that's a woman who collects for the whole city and lets nobody, and I mean nobody, collect for her. The Love is real, boss. The Tax is a lie. Twenty-two is what you charge strangers. This section says what you charge yourself, and it isn't twenty-two."

THE COLLECTOR: P 68 (-2) L 87 (-1) T 28 (+6) │ PLT 64.8 ▼

WREN: P 58 (+3) L 75 (+2) T 33 (-1) │ PLT 54.0 ▲

*Analyst: Role reversal, same fixed rite: the apprentice reads the Collector, and being read costs the Collector Tax up front — the first honest invoice she's received in years.*

**Beat 2 — COLLECTING FOR EVERYONE**

WREN: "Page two of your section. It's a list. Every soul you carried home for somebody else — and in the margin, in your own handwriting, one word, over and over: 'later.' Later, you'll rest. Later, you'll grieve your own page. Later, somebody will do for you what you do at that desk. Boss. How long has 'later' been compounding?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Since before you were shelving, baby."

WREN: "That's not an answer. That's a tenure."

THE COLLECTOR: *[quiet]* "The room needed a collector who doesn't flinch. Flinching is expensive. So I put mine in the margin and kept the desk. You don't survive this work by being carried, Wren. You survive it by being the one who carries."

WREN: "Write that down, somebody — oh wait. The book already did. It's under Tax."

THE COLLECTOR: P 65 (-3) L 87 (—) T 33 (+5) │ PLT 61.6 ▼

WREN: P 60 (+2) L 78 (+3) T 32 (-1) │ PLT 56.4 ▲

*Analyst: The debt has no creditor — that's what makes it heavy. She owes it to the version of herself she kept postponing, and the margin word "later" has been accruing Tax the whole book.*

**Beat 3 — LET NO ONE CARRY IT**

WREN: "Page three. Mama Lou, at the family table, said it plainer than this book dares: 'You collect for everyone and let no one collect for you.' She read you at her own table, boss. In front of the greens."

THE COLLECTOR: "Mama Lou reads everybody in front of the greens. It's her ministry."

WREN: "And she was right, and you knew it, and you came back here and opened everybody's book but this section. I dust this room. I know which pages stick. This one sticks because it's the only one in the building that's scared."

THE COLLECTOR: "Careful, apprentice."

WREN: "No. Careful is your Tax talking. I'm the reader tonight — you said the reader doesn't flinch."

THE COLLECTOR: P 64 (-1) L 84 (-3) T 37 (+4) │ PLT 58.4 ▼

WREN: P 61 (+1) L 82 (+4) T 30 (-2) │ PLT 59.2 ▲

*Analyst: The dip bottoms here: the Collector's Love falls as the defense does — naming the fear costs her the story she told about her own strength. Wren passes her on the board, and neither of them looks away.*

**Beat 4 — MAMA LOU'S SENTENCE**

THE COLLECTOR: "You want the truth in the room? Here it is, apprentice, and you may write it in your neat hand. If I let the room carry me, I have to find out whether it can. And if it can't — I have to keep collecting anyway, knowing. Not knowing has been cheaper. That's the whole confession. Put it in the book next to 'later' and underline them both."

WREN: "Or— hear the reader out — you could find out it can. This room carried Pearl. It carried Felix, and Felix was paperwork. It's carrying me and I arrived as a hiding place with a duster. Boss, the room isn't the risk. The not-knowing is the bill. You've been paying the expensive option and calling it strength."

THE COLLECTOR: *[a long breath]* "…Read the settlement, Wren."

THE COLLECTOR: P 66 (+2) L 86 (+2) T 35 (-2) │ PLT 60.8 ▲

WREN: P 63 (+2) L 84 (+2) T 29 (-1) │ PLT 61.2 ▲

*Analyst: Mama Lou's sentence, carried from the family table into this room, does its work: the Collector stops defending the debt and starts pricing its alternative. Her numbers turn — before the settlement is even spoken.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**

WREN: "The settlement, plain — like you taught me. The Collector's debt is this: collecting for everyone, collected by no one, 'later' written in the margin of her own life until the margin ran out. It is named. And it is collected — not by the scythe, not by the score. By the room. Tonight, for one page, the room carries the Collector. No desk. No standing. Sit in the good chair, boss, and be carried. That's the payment. Being carried is the payment."

THE COLLECTOR: "And the receipt? There's always a receipt, Wren. I taught you that too."

WREN: *[taking a small tagged Mendie soul from the shelf and placing it in the Collector's hands — the Collector, for once, empty of Drippy, who sits watching from the throne]* "Mendie. The mender, held by the woman who mends everybody. Tag stays on. When it feels heavy, read your page. When it feels light — that's us, doing the job right."

THE COLLECTOR: *[holding it, and — for the first time in ten chapters — simply sitting]* "…It feels light already. Put that in the book."

THE COLLECTOR: *[quiet]* "Settled."

THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+8) L 98 (+12) T 25 (-10) │ PLT 72.8 ▲

WREN: P 67 (+4) L 90 (+6) T 25 (-4) │ PLT 66.8 ▲

*Analyst: The biggest PLT rise in the book, and it belongs to the Collector — because her settlement required the one move she never makes: receiving. Love up twelve, Tax down ten, paid in full by being carried for exactly one page.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 72.8 · P 74 · L 98 · T 25 — ◆◆◆◆ "CARRIED, AT LAST"
WREN — PLT 66.8 · P 67 · L 90 · T 25 — ◆◆◆ "THE READER RISES"
**THE MOVE — "THE READER NEVER READ"**
*The Collector's play: She collects for everyone and lets no one collect for her, being the reader of every page so no page is ever read to her. The maxim: "To read every page and exempt your own is to make a gospel with one soul left unsaved — yours." The Collector's answer: LET THE ROOM CARRY HER — She submits to her own book, sits in the good chair, and lets the room carry her for one page.*
**Takeaway:** *"Even the Collector has a page — and being carried is a payment too."*

**Beats:** 1. THE READING — Wren reads the Collector's holding aloud: P 70, L 88, T 22, PLT 68.4 — and calls the Tax a lie. 2. COLLECTING FOR EVERYONE — The margin word "later," written over and over beside every soul she carried for others, is priced as compounding debt. 3. LET NO ONE CARRY IT — Mama Lou's family-table sentence lands in the room; the Collector's numbers bottom out as the defense falls. 4. MAMA LOU'S SENTENCE — The confession: not knowing whether the room could carry her was billed as cheaper than finding out. 5. THE SETTLEMENT — The room carries the Collector for one page; Wren hands her a tagged Mendie soul as her receipt, and her PLT posts the biggest rise in the book.

BEING CARRIED IS THE PAYMENT

### TC10.4 — The Last Soul Due

*The Collection Room, last hour of the night — pink-and-teal throne-room light gone low and golden, the "COLLECTED SOULS — SECTION 222" plaque above shelves of tagged souls (Spike, Drippy, Mendie, Denim Patch, Ember Stripe, Pixel Heart, Bell Paws, Nightwing Dawn, Listen), the receipt wall glowing file by file. THE COLLECTOR in full regalia — pink-and-gold polka-dot motif, crown-energy headpiece, ceremonial scythe standing by the door, Drippy in her arm, grey three-eyed Spike bookbag on her back, pink collection book open to its final page — BETTY PLUSH / 222 / PLT branding on the wall and the book's last leaf. An old woman waits in the doorway, holding nothing but a folded, forty-year-old memory.*

**In the room:** THE COLLECTOR (soul collector, Soul Collection Agency) · MISS VERA LANE (an old woman carrying a forty-year debt of gratitude to a stranger whose name she never learned)

**OPENING HOLDING**

THE COLLECTOR — P 70 · L 88 · T 22 │ PLT 68.4

MISS VERA LANE — P 58 · L 82 · T 30 │ PLT 58.0

**Beat 1 — THE READING**

MISS VERA LANE: "I know it's late, and I know I'm the last page. I checked with the girl at the wall. She said the last page is the one that matters, so I wore my good shoes."

THE COLLECTOR: "Miss Vera Lane. The reading, like we do everybody — last page, same respect. Profit fifty-eight, Love eighty-two, Tax thirty. PLT fifty-eight point zero. Miss Vera, that's not a debtor's page. That's a bank with the vault door open and nobody walking in. What is a woman with Love at eighty-two doing in my Collection Room at closing time?"

MISS VERA LANE: "Owing, Collector. Owing the oldest kind."

THE COLLECTOR: P 71 (+1) L 90 (+2) T 21 (-1) │ PLT 70.0 ▲

MISS VERA LANE: P 57 (-1) L 83 (+1) T 32 (+2) │ PLT 57.2 ▼

*Analyst: The final reading of the book finds no delinquency at all — a surplus with a stone in it. Vera's Tax ticks up at the threshold: forty years is a long time to approach a door.*

**Beat 2 — FORTY YEARS OF INTEREST**

MISS VERA LANE: "Forty years ago I was a girl at a bus stop in the rain with a baby, a broken bag, and exactly no fare. A woman I'd never seen paid the driver, gave me her umbrella, and fixed my bag strap with a safety pin she took off her own dress. She got off two stops later. I never learned her name. I have owed a thank-you for forty years, Collector, and gratitude compounds worse than money. Money you can mail. This— this needs a witness."

THE COLLECTOR: "Forty years of interest on an umbrella and a safety pin."

MISS VERA LANE: "And a fare. Don't short her, she's not here to correct the record — that's the whole problem. She's not here. So I brought the debt to the only office that delivers to people you can't find."

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (+2) L 91 (+1) T 21 (—) │ PLT 71.2 ▲

MISS VERA LANE: P 59 (+2) L 86 (+3) T 30 (-2) │ PLT 60.0 ▲

*Analyst: Vera's debt is real precisely because it isn't owed to a ledger — it's owed to a moment. Her Love climbs as she tells it in full: gratitude, itemized at last, stops being a weight and starts being an errand.*

**Beat 3 — THE NAME SHE NEVER LEARNED**

THE COLLECTOR: "The book's rule, Miss Vera: we collect what's named. You owe a woman with no name, at a bus stop, forty years gone. Describe her into the book. Everything. The book is better at faces than people think."

MISS VERA LANE: "Pink scarf. Laugh like a screen door — happy noise, a little squeak in it. A flowered bag with — Lord, I remember the bag better than the woman. A bunny on it. Pink, dripping like it had walked out of a candy store in the rain—"

THE COLLECTOR: *[very still]* "Say that again. The bag."

MISS VERA LANE: "A pink bunny bag, dripping pink, like candy. And the woman told the driver — I wrote it down once, it's the only sentence of hers I own — she said, 'We collect each other out here. Now drive.'"

THE COLLECTOR: P 73 (—) L 93 (+2) T 20 (-1) │ PLT 72.4 ▲

MISS VERA LANE: P 60 (+1) L 89 (+3) T 29 (-1) │ PLT 62.0 ▲

*Analyst: A pink drip bunny, a safety pin, and a sentence that sounds like the Agency's first draft of itself. The Collector recognizes the description before she can prove it — and her Love moves first, the way it does when a file is about to open itself.*

**Beat 4 — THE WALL REMEMBERS**

THE COLLECTOR: "Wren. Third shelf. The file from this morning — the smiling one. Read the tag."

WREN: *[from the wall]* "Ember Stripe file. Pearl Adeyemi. Settled. Note in her page, in her own words: 'Forty years of catching strangers at bus stops. Paid my own page off one umbrella at a time.' Boss — boss, it's her. Pink scarf in the file photo. And she's— her receipt soul's been on this wall all day, smiling at the door."

MISS VERA LANE: "She's in this room? She's been in this room the whole time I was telling it?"

THE COLLECTOR: "Settled souls stay on the wall till their pages are finished, Miss Vera. And a page isn't finished while somebody's still carrying a thank-you for it. Pearl's file smiled all morning. Now we know who it was smiling at."

THE COLLECTOR: P 74 (+1) L 94 (+1) T 19 (-1) │ PLT 73.6 ▲

MISS VERA LANE: P 63 (+3) L 93 (+4) T 26 (-3) │ PLT 66.0 ▲

*Analyst: The receipt wall pays off its first scene: the smiling file was a delivery address. The stranger was findable all along — she was collected, settled, and shelved with her page held open for exactly this knock.*

**Beat 5 — THE SETTLEMENT**

THE COLLECTOR: "The settlement, plain — the last one tonight, so we say it right. Vera Lane owes a thank-you, forty years standing, to Pearl Adeyemi, a stranger. The debt is not forgiven. Forgiving would waste it. The debt is collected — I take it from your book, tonight, witnessed, on the record — and the Agency delivers it onward, to Pearl's page, where it lands as what it always was: proof that her umbrella is still traveling. Say it to the file, Miss Vera. She's collected. She can hear a settled thing."

MISS VERA LANE: *[to the Ember Stripe soul on the shelf, steady as a church]* "Pearl. I never got your name, so I kept your sentence instead. The baby grew up. The fare got paid forward about four hundred times, but the first one was yours. Thank you. It took me forty years to deliver, and I delivered it to a throne room. You always did travel first class."

THE COLLECTOR: "Collected and delivered. And your receipt, Miss Vera —" *[she takes a small tagged Nightwing Dawn soul from the shelf and places it in Vera's hands]* "— Nightwing Dawn. For a debt carried faithfully through the dark till morning. Tag stays on. Your page, and Pearl's, close together. That's rarer than you know."

THE COLLECTOR: *[quiet]* "Settled."

THE COLLECTOR: P 77 (+3) L 97 (+3) T 17 (-2) │ PLT 76.8 ▲

MISS VERA LANE: P 68 (+5) L 98 (+5) T 21 (-5) │ PLT 72.0 ▲

*Analyst: The last collection of the night collects a thank-you and delivers it onward — the Agency's whole machinery, scythe and shelves and scores, used for gratitude. Vera's settlement is the thesis with a name and a bus stop: nothing was taken from her. Something forty years heavy was, finally, settled.*

**FINAL SCORE**
THE COLLECTOR — PLT 76.8 · P 77 · L 97 · T 17 — ◆◆◆◆ "THE LAST PAGE, KEPT"
MISS VERA LANE — PLT 72.0 · P 68 · L 98 · T 21 — ◆◆◆◆ "DELIVERED AT LAST"
**THE MOVE — "THE KEPT THANK-YOU"**
*Miss Vera Lane's play: She guarded an unspoken thank-you for forty years as if gratitude kept too long stays hers, hoarding a debt of gratitude until it became the heaviest thing she owns. The maxim: "A thank-you held too long in silence stops being a gift kept safe and becomes a weight kept alone." The Collector's answer: COLLECT AND DELIVER ONWARD — She takes the thank-you from Vera's book, witnessed and on the record, and delivers it onward to Pearl's page where it always belonged.*
**Takeaway:** *"A collected soul isn't taken — it's settled."*

**Beats:** 1. THE READING — The Collector reads Vera's holding aloud: P 58, L 82, T 30, PLT 58.0 — not a debtor's page, a bank with the vault open. 2. FORTY YEARS OF INTEREST — Vera itemizes the debt: a fare, an umbrella, a safety pin off a stranger's own dress, at a rainy bus stop forty years ago. 3. THE NAME SHE NEVER LEARNED — She describes the stranger: pink scarf, screen-door laugh, a pink dripping bunny bag, and one kept sentence — "We collect each other out here. Now drive." 4. THE WALL REMEMBERS — Wren finds her on the receipt wall: Pearl Adeyemi, the Ember Stripe file, smiling at the door all morning. 5. THE SETTLEMENT — The thank-you is collected from Vera's book and delivered onward to Pearl's page; Vera receives a tagged Nightwing Dawn soul as her receipt, and both pages close together.

## CLOSING — The Book Stays Open

The rounds continue. Tomorrow there will be another corner, another counter, another family table, another name read aloud in a room that goes quiet for it — because every conversation has a score, whether anyone writes it down or not. The Collector's whole gospel is only that somebody should write it down honestly, read it back kindly, and name the settlement plain.

The receipt wall glows a little after closing. Wren will dust it in the morning, a settled woman dusting settled souls, and somewhere on the third shelf an Ember Stripe file and a Nightwing Dawn receipt will sit in each other's light — a thank-you, delivered; a collector, carried; an apprentice, read; a book that admitted it could be wrong about the record and was never wrong about a single person in it.

She hangs the ceremonial scythe by the door, where it belongs — a tool for opening pages, never for taking them. Drippy goes on the throne. The Spike bookbag goes on its hook under the plaque, three eyes closed at last. The pink collection book stays on the desk, open, because a finished book is just a book whose next page hasn't knocked yet.

We collect souls. Now we sell souls.

— Craig Jones · PLT Press
